Compact canonical constructors (`public Point { ... }`, no parameter list, STYLE_JAVA17.md §1 "ordinary method-body rules, not getter/setter one-liner rules") needed genuinely new detection in the same method, added as `isCompactConstructorBrace`. **Regression caught during verification, not by the user**: the first version of `isCompactConstructorBrace` matched *any* bare `IDENTIFIER` directly before `{` not preceded by `new`, which wrongly fired on the pre-existing, unrelated `class Foo implements Bar {` shape too (`Bar` is just as bare an IDENTIFIER as a constructor name) -- caught by diffing behavior against the pristine pre-change build on a small regression battery, not by any explicit instruction. Fixed by walking backward from the candidate name, over only access modifiers (`public`/`private`/`protected` -- the sole legal compact-constructor prefix), and requiring what remains to be a genuine member/scope boundary (`}`, `;`, `{`, or start of file); `implements`/`extends`/`,` never satisfy that, so they now correctly fail the check without needing `Token.name` (which is null for both the true- and false-positive shape here, since the tokenizer's own lookback has the identical blind spot through an arbitrary-length `implements`/`extends` clause -- a separate, pre-existing, undocumented Tier-1 gap, left untouched as out of scope for this item). Component-list line-breaking required no new code: `ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass`'s existing `)`-then-`{` signature detection already matches `record Name(...) {` and renders it via §8's signature rules, confirmed with a long component list wrapping one-component-per-line with `)` back at the `record` keyword's own column. One further documented gap, deliberately left as-is rather than fixed: a record with *both* a long component list *and* an `implements` clause does not get its component list wrapped, because `applySignaturePass` requires `)` directly before `{` just like the original `enforceMethodDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle` did -- this is the exact same "trailing qualifier between `)` and `{` blocks the wrap/restructure pass" posture already established and documented for a method's own `throws` clause, so extending past it here would be inconsistent with that precedent, not an oversight. Verified via a standalone harness (no `Main.java` yet): basic record (K&R brace, blank lines, `// record Point` closing comment, idempotent), record with `implements` (same, label still `record Point` not `implements Point`), compact constructor with an already-broken-onto-its-own-line brace and with a same-line brace needing Allman conversion, package-private compact constructor (no modifier), enum constant body with no constructor-arg parens (`ACTIVE { ... }`, the original false-positive case) confirmed still K&R/untouched, nested record inside a class, generic record name (no crash, just untagged), multiple-interface `implements` clause, long component list with and without a trailing `implements` clause, and a small regression battery (plain `class ... implements`, plain `interface ... extends`, prior `sealed`/`permits` feature, method `throws` clause, a variable literally named `record`) diffed byte-for-byte against the pristine pre-change build to confirm no behavior changed outside record's own new cases. |
| RDD_KEY_85 | concepts/`requires` implementation in `CppSpecificRule.java` | Two sub-tasks, both additive. (1) `concept` K&R brace: `BlockStructureRule.enforceKAndRBraceStyle` already places all `{` K&R by default; `enforceFunctionDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle` then moves only function-definition braces (those directly preceded by `)` after a candidate signature name) back to Allman. A `concept`'s `= requires { }` brace is preceded by `)` of the `requires(T t)` parameter list, which is preceded by `requires` -- a KEYWORD, not an IDENTIFIER -- so `isCandidateSignatureName` returns false and the Allman pass leaves it untouched. Net result: K&R is already correct for `concept` bodies with no new code. Verify via smoke test before concluding no-op. (2) `requires` and `concept` are NOT in `KEYWORDS_CPP` and `NAMED_CONSTRUCT_CPP` (confirmed from source) -- both must be added: add `"requires"` and `"concept"` to `KEYWORDS_CPP` in `TokenizerCore.java`; add `"concept"` to `NAMED_CONSTRUCT_CPP` so that `concept Drawable = ...` gets a `// concept Drawable` closing comment and blank-line insertion, same as `class`/`struct`. (3) Trailing `requires` clause placement: new `enforceRequiresClausePlacement` method in `CppSpecificRule.java`. Detection: scan for KEYWORD token with text `"requires"`. Disambiguate trailing clause from requirements expression body by looking at the previous significant token -- if it is `)`, it is a trailing clause (follows a function/template parameter list); if it is `=` or anything else, it is a requirements expression body, leave entirely untouched. For each identified trailing-clause `requires`: (a) find the preceding `)` (the parameter list close); (b) collect the full `requires ...` clause tokens up to but not including the `{` or `;` that terminates the signature; (c) if `) requires <clause>` all fits on one line within 100 chars → ensure `requires` is on the same line as `)`, done; (d) if combined exceeds 100 chars → `requires` goes on its own line indented one level under the function name's line-leading indent (reuse existing `lineIndent` helper). Nested compound requirements inside a `requires { }` body are left completely untouched -- the scan stops at `{`. Wire `enforceRequiresClausePlacement` into `Formatter.formatOne`'s `isCpp` Phase 1 block after `enforceEmptyParameterList` (line 42). All helpers needed (`prevSignificantIndex`, `nextSignificantIndex`, `lineIndent`, `matchParenBackward`) already exist in `CppSpecificRule.java`. (1)'s no-op claim verified true via smoke test (idempotent, byte-identical K&R `requires(T t) { ... }` body). **Gap caught during verification, not anticipated by the plan above**: adding `"concept"` to `NAMED_CONSTRUCT_CPP` alone did not produce the closing comment, because `TokenizerCore.computeConstructName()`'s 2-token lookback (same limitation already documented for `record` in RDD_KEY_2) can't see across `concept Name = requires(params)` to reach the brace -- it saw only `)`/whatever token directly preceded `{`, found no `concept`+name pair, and tagged the brace unnamed. No stop-and-ask needed -- resolved the same way RDD_KEY_2 was, by extending detection additively, not by changing already-COMPLETE behavior. Fix, mirroring `record`'s `bracketNameStack`/`pendingRecordName` precedent but simpler (no paren-balancing needed, since nothing between `concept Name =` and the body's own `{` can open another brace): new `pendingConceptName` field, armed in `TokenizerCore.trackSignificant` when an `=` token's two immediately-preceding significant tokens are KEYWORD `concept` + an IDENTIFIER (new helper `computeConceptHeaderName`, parallel to the existing `computeRecordHeaderName`), consumed by `emitOpenBrace` (checked between `pendingRecordName` and the `computeConstructName()` fallback). Cleared without effect on a `;` reached before any `{` -- handles a body-less concept (`concept Integral = std::is_integral_v<T>;`) without misattributing the name to a later, unrelated brace; confirmed via a smoke test with exactly this shape followed by an ordinary function, which renders and Allman-converts normally with no `concept` mistagging. A second, independent gap existed in `BlockStructureRule.classifyNamed` (closing-comment label) for the same reason `record` needed `findRecordComponentListClose` in RDD_KEY_2 -- the token directly before `{` (`)` of the `requires(...)` parameter list) isn't the construct's own name, so the generic `kwIdx`/`identIdx` 2-token lookback produced a bare `Drawable` label instead of `concept Drawable`. Fixed with a new `isConceptRequiresExpressionBody` backward-scanner (predecessor chain `) requires ( ) = name concept`, via the same `matchOpenBackward` used by `findRecordComponentListClose`), checked *before* `findRecordComponentListClose` in `classifyNamed` -- required ordering, since that record check unconditionally matches on the immediate `)` predecessor with no keyword-specific gate and would otherwise misclassify this exact shape first (confirmed by trace: it resolved to a non-`record` keyword and fell through to the bare-name return, never reaching the concept check below it). Verified end-to-end via smoke tests: `concept` definition gets `// concept Drawable` and forced blank-lines-after-`{`/before-`}` (STYLE.md §7's named-construct rule, confirmed correct rather than a bug once cross-checked against that section), idempotent; re-ran the original (1)/(3) smoke tests afterward with no change in output. |
| RDD_KEY_86 | `MiscRule.java` call/declaration line-breaking (options 1 and 2) -- architecture from source inspection | `parseSignature` calls `significantOnly()` at the top, which strips WHITESPACE, NEWLINE, COMMENT_LINE, and COMMENT_BLOCK tokens before any parsing. This has two consequences: (1) The checklist item "verify `parseSignature` bails on comment tokens between params" is RESOLVED as a no-op -- `parseSignature` silently ignores comments rather than bailing. This is safe for option 1 (all args dropped to one line -- comments in param lists are rare in calls/forward-decls, and the option 2 preamble-comment guard handles the important case). (2) Option 2 (preserve groups + align) MUST NOT go through `parseSignature` at all -- it needs to preserve the original line break and comment structure. Option 2 operates directly on the raw token stream, not through `Signature`/`Param`. Architecture per option: **Option 1 (dropped form):** Can reuse `parseSignature` + a new `renderDropped(Signature sig, int indentLevel, String indentStyle)` method alongside the existing `render()`. `renderDropped` joins all params on one indented line (no column alignment), preceded by a newline+indent, with `)` on its own line. Fit check: render the single params line, measure `indentLevel * INDENT_WIDTH + 1 + params.length()` -- if ≤ 100, use dropped; else fall back to existing `render()` (one-per-line). Called from a new `enforceCallLineBreaking` whole-file pass wired into `Formatter.formatOne` Phase 1 (after `enforceEmptyParameterList` for C++, after `enforcePermitsClauseLineBreaking` for Java, before `formatNonInlineSwitches` for both). Detection: scan for `(` preceded by an IDENTIFIER (same `isCandidateSignatureName` signal), NOT followed by `{` (excludes function definitions -- those are already handled by `ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass`). Collect tokens from the candidate name through the matching `)`. **Option 2 (preserve groups):** Separate method `enforceCallGroupPreservation`. Detection: same `(` scan, not followed by `{`. Check if the param list is already multi-line (contains NEWLINE between `(` and `)`). If yes: scan for leading preamble comment (COMMENT token before any arg on the first line) -- if found, leave entire arg list untouched. Otherwise: normalize spacing around `,` within each line (for calls), or apply `parseDeclaration`/`ColumnGrid` per group line (for forward declarations). Ensure `)` is on its own line. Comment-only lines between groups are left in place. Both options are wired in the same new `enforceCallLineBreaking` pass -- option 2 applied first (multi-line source, mechanically), then option 1 fallback (inline source exceeding 100 chars, dropped or one-per-line). |
| RDD_KEY_87 | `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` -- implementation scope decisions made while building RDD_KEY_4's design (calls vs. declarations, nesting, comments, declaration-grid scope), plus one real bug found and fixed during smoke testing | Implemented as a single whole-file pass, `enforceCallLineBreaking`, scanning for `(` preceded by an IDENTIFIER with a matching `)` not followed by `{` (excludes definitions, already owned by `ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass`). Four scope decisions made without needing to stop and ask (none change already-COMPLETE behavior, all are filling in gaps RDD_KEY_4 left open): (1) **Nesting** -- once a `(` is recognized as a candidate (valid IDENTIFIER+matching-`)`), its entire interior is claimed (`scanCursor = closeIdx + 1`) and skipped for the rest of the scan, regardless of whether the candidate is ultimately rewritten or left alone (e.g. because it fits inline) -- a nested call/declaration inside another's argument list is never independently processed in the same pass; this is consistent with "no semantic grouping, no AI" (RDD_EXT_5/Step 2 NOT FEASIBLE) -- there is no mechanism to re-visit a nested candidate with different context once the outer one has decided its own layout. (2) **Comments** -- any comment token anywhere between a candidate's `(` and `)` disqualifies the *entire* candidate, left byte-for-byte untouched; this implements only the simplest case of RDD_EXT_6's comment rules (which distinguish trailing/comment-only-line/inline-block/leading-preamble) -- the fuller per-case handling is a documented, deliberate gap, not yet implemented, since no current STYLE.md worked example for *this* pass (as opposed to signature breaking) exercises the distinction and getting it wrong risks corrupting working code (same risk-calculus precedent as `CppSpecificRule`'s `(void)`/`()` two-direction split). (3) **Call vs. declaration classification** -- `parseSignature` is attempted on the candidate's `name(...)` span; success (typed params parse cleanly) means forward declaration, routed through the typed `Signature`/`Param` rendering machinery; failure means plain call, routed through raw per-argument token handling via `splitTopLevelCommas`. (4) **Declaration preserve-groups grid scope** -- per user direction (asked via AskUserQuestion since `DeclarationAlignmentRule.render()` only renders one `Declaration` per output line and could not be reused as-is for RDD_EXT_4's multi-param-per-line worked example): wrote new alignment logic specific to this case (`renderDeclarationPreserveGroups`), grouping params by original physical line and aligning by slot position across lines via `ColumnGrid` (which already supports variable-width rows, exempting each row's own last cell from padding) -- matches the spec exactly, no comment column needed since decision (2) above already disqualifies any candidate with a comment before this renderer is ever reached. **Bug found and fixed during smoke testing (real correctness issue, not a documented gap):** `renderCallDropped`/`renderCallOnePerLine`/`renderCallPreserveGroups` originally flattened argument token spans through the shared `renderTokens` helper (the same one `render()`/`renderParamsInline` use for typed param lists). `renderTokens`'s tight-attachment rules (`isTightToken`) only special-case `,`/`[`/`]`/`*`/`&`/`::`/angle-brackets -- it has never needed to know about `(`/`)` adjacency, because every *existing* caller's token lists are typed signature fragments (qualifiers, type names, array-size brackets) that never literally contain a call's parens. Once `enforceCallLineBreaking` started passing arbitrary *expression* token spans (real call arguments, which may themselves contain a nested call) through it, a nested call inside a rewritten outer candidate got its own parens spread apart by ordinary single-space joining: source `bar(1,  2)` as an argument rendered as `bar ( 1, 2 )` once the outer call needed breaking (confirmed reproducible only when the outer candidate is actually rewritten -- an outer call that fits inline never routes its arguments through any renderer, so nesting stays perfectly verbatim in that case, which is why earlier smoke tests calling out "nested call left verbatim when outer fits" passed while this one did not). Considered and rejected: extending `renderTokens`'s tight-rules to cover `(`/`)` -- correct tightness for a paren depends on whether it's a call paren (tight after an identifier) or a grouping/operator paren (`x + (a + b)`, needs a leading space), a distinction `renderTokens`'s flat token-by-token pass has no way to make reliably, and getting it wrong would be a regression in the opposite direction. Fix actually applied: new `collapseTokensToOneLine(List<Token>)` helper (whitespace/newline-run-collapsing, same precedent as the existing index-range `collapseToOneLine`, but operating on a detached sublist) used in place of `renderTokens` for all three call-argument renderers; top-level sibling arguments (found via `splitTopLevelCommas`, called directly on the raw token slice -- it already tracks paren/bracket/angle depth itself, so no `significantOnly()` pre-pass is needed) are joined with an explicit normalized `", "`, while each argument's own internal tokens (which may include a nested, unanalyzed call) are reproduced with their original spacing preserved (whitespace runs collapsed, nothing new inserted) rather than actively re-spaced -- consistent with decision (1)'s "claim and skip" posture: a nested candidate's interior was never independently analyzed, so it should ride along unprocessed rather than being normalized as if it had been. Declaration renderers (`renderDropped`/`renderOnePerLine`/`renderDeclarationPreserveGroups`) were not affected and needed no change -- their token lists are typed (`Param.typeTokens`/name/`sizeTokens`), which by construction never contain a literal `(`/`)`, so they remain correctly routed through `renderTokens`. Verified via direct smoke testing (`Formatter.formatOne`, bypassing not-yet-built `Main.java`) across 13 scenarios spanning C++ and Java, both calls and forward declarations, all four candidate forms, nesting (both outer-fits and outer-rewritten cases), comment bail-out, split-across-line leading-comma normalization, and the explicit-`(void)`-never-broken case (confirmed to be pre-existing `CppSpecificRule.enforceEmptyParameterList` behavior unrelated to this pass, not a bug -- that method already converts C++ `(void)` to `()` unconditionally per STYLE_C_CPP.md §1, well before `enforceCallLineBreaking` ever sees the candidate, so `enforceCallLineBreaking` correctly never has anything left to do there). All 13 scenarios pass after the fix; `make clean && make` succeeds with no warnings or errors. |
| RDD_KEY_88 | `Main.java` implementation (Step 1.5) -- CLI parsing, config resolution, standalone indent-style temp-cache, server auto-connect/delegate, `--server`/`--stop`, output modes, exit codes -- plus one real bug found and fixed via smoke testing, and two pre-existing gaps discovered but left unfixed as out of scope | Implementation follows the Step 1.5 spec for the temp-file indent-style cache (SHA-256 hex of boundary dir path, `/tmp/jxmake-code-formatter-indent-<hash>.cache`, content = style + `\n` + boundary dir `lastModified` ms, pre-populated single-entry map passed to `IndentationDetector.detect()` on a hit). Three small additive changes to other already-COMPLETE files were needed, none changing existing behavior for any pre-existing caller: (1) `IndentationDetector.findBoundaryDir` widened `private`→`public static` so `Main` can compute the same boundary dir `detect()` would have used, for the cache key/freshness check -- `detect()` itself, the method's only existing caller, is unaffected. (2) Added `ServerMode.findRunningServerPort()` (new public method, read-only, reuses the existing private `readLockfilePid`/`isProcessAlive` helpers) so `Main`'s server-auto-connect logic doesn't have to duplicate the lockfile path/format -- a divergence risk if `Main` had its own copy and the two drifted. (3) Added `ServerMode.stop()` (new public method implementing RDD_KEY_73's full stop protocol: POST `/shutdown` with short timeout, poll for lockfile removal, fall back to a new `forceKill()` helper using the same reflective-`ProcessHandle`-with-Java-8-`kill -9`-fallback pattern as the existing `isProcessAlive`) for the same reason -- `ServerMode` already owns the lockfile path constant and PID-liveness logic, so it is the natural owner of the matching stop logic too, not `Main`. **Real bug found and fixed via smoke testing:** the first version of `Main.main()` called `System.exit(run(args))` unconditionally. For `--server`, `ServerMode.start()` successfully bound the HTTP listener and returned -- but the very next line, `System.exit(...)`, immediately tore down the whole JVM (including the just-spawned listener threads) before any client could ever connect; observed directly as a background shell job reporting `Done` within the same second it printed `server listening on port ...`, instead of staying `Running`. Root cause: `ServerMode.start()` was `void`, giving the caller no way to distinguish "freshly started, must stay alive until `/shutdown` calls `System.exit` itself" from "nothing to do, already running or failed, safe to exit now" -- and `ServerMode.start()` had no caller anywhere in the codebase before this task, so changing its return type to `boolean` (true = freshly started; false = already running or startup failed) is not a behavior change to any working code path, only a new way for the one new caller to observe an outcome the method already determined internally. `Main.run()` now returns a private sentinel (`SERVER_STARTED_KEEP_ALIVE = -1`, distinct from the three real non-negative exit codes) in the freshly-started case, and `Main.main()` skips `System.exit` only then. Verified via smoke test: `--server` now stays in the background (`Running`, not `Done`), a second `--server` call against the same port correctly reports "already running" and exits 0, a client file format request auto-connects and succeeds, and `--stop` cleanly terminates it and removes the lockfile, idempotently. **`--diff` output mode** required a unified-diff implementation; no existing diff utility exists anywhere in this codebase, and shelling out to the system `diff` binary was rejected as inconsistent with the project's established no-shell-out-where-avoidable posture (already evidenced by `ServerMode.isProcessAlive`'s Linux-`/proc`-before-`kill -0`-shell-out fallback ordering). Implemented as a self-contained line-level LCS diff (`computeDiffRuns`, O(n·m) dynamic programming -- acceptable for source-file-sized inputs) producing minimal equal/delete/insert runs, then wrapped into a *single* hunk per file spanning from the first to the last changed run with context clamped to 3 lines at each end -- a deliberate simplification versus GNU `diff -u`'s behavior of splitting widely-separated change clusters into multiple hunks; still fully correct unified-diff output, just less terse for files with several far-apart edits, and far simpler to verify correct by hand than multi-hunk grouping logic. Standard "no newline at end of file" marker is not implemented (narrow edge case, no spec requirement, and source files in this project always end with a trailing newline by the time they reach this tool). **Two pre-existing gaps discovered via smoke testing, deliberately left unfixed** (not itemized in the Step 1.5 checklist, and both are existing-logic changes to already-COMPLETE files -- flagged to the user rather than silently fixed, per the Hard Constraint): (1) `ServerMode.FormatHandler.handle()` passes `config.indentStyle()` straight into `Formatter.formatOne` with no `"auto"`-resolution step (the kind `Main.formatStandalone()` now does via the temp-cache), so any project configured with `indent-style = auto` going through the *server* path throws `IllegalArgumentException` inside `MiscRule.convertIndentation` and the request fails with a 500 -- reproduced directly in smoke testing; `Main`'s server-delegation-failure-falls-back-to-standalone resilience (added independently, for general robustness against a server dying mid-request) happens to mask this in practice for CLI users, but the server-side bug itself is unfixed. (2) `Config.lineEndings()` was, before this task, read by no code anywhere (`grep` across `Formatter.java` and all rule classes confirmed zero references) -- `Main.applyLineEndings()` is the first consumer, applied only on the standalone/in-process formatting path; `ServerMode.FormatHandler` still does not apply it, so server-delegated formatting never normalizes line endings regardless of config. Both gaps are isolated to `ServerMode.java`'s existing `FormatHandler`; fixing them was judged out of scope for "implement `Main.java`" and is recorded here for a future task. Verified via manual smoke testing (no automated test harness exists yet): CLI usage errors (no args, unknown flag, exit 2), `--check` before/after formatting (exit 1 then 0), `--diff` against an unformatted file, in-place formatting, `--out DIR` (original left untouched, formatted copy written under `DIR/<filename>`), `indent-style = auto` end-to-end through the temp-cache (tabs detected and applied, cache file written with the correct two-line format), full `--server`/idempotent-restart/auto-connect-format/`--stop`/idempotent-restop lifecycle, and a 19-file `--check` sweep of the formatter's own `src/` tree (all exit 1 with zero stderr output, confirming no crashes -- the dogfood checklist items proper, including whether the resulting diffs are sane and whether `make` still succeeds after an in-place run, remain open and are intentionally not yet attempted pending user direction on scope). |
| RDD_KEY_89 | `combined_inp.java` dogfood -- three independent bugs: (1) §15 sentence-detection for consecutive `//` line comments now groups them exactly like an equivalent multi-line `/* */` block (dot count across the whole adjacent-line group decides whether the last line's trailing period is stripped), instead of deciding per-line in isolation -- fixes a false strip when a multi-line prose paragraph's other lines also contain dots. (2) New rule (no prior `STYLE_JAVA.md` worked example): a Java enum body with trailing members after its constant list detaches the constant-list-terminating `;` onto its own line with a blank line before and after it (`CONST_A, CONST_B` / blank / `;` / blank / first member) -- user-confirmed via chat; required updating `java_core_out.java`'s previously-passing enum fixture (which had encoded the opposite, compact shape) to match. (3) `MiscRule.isFunctionBodyBrace`'s post-paren-qualifier skip only recognized the single-token `throws` keyword, not the exception name(s) following it, so a method with a `throws ExceptionType` clause was never recognized as a function body and `insertBlankLineBeforeReturn` silently never fired for it -- fixed with a new clause-skipping helper. |
| RDD_KEY_90 | Task A (`JXM_CFMT_DIS`/`ENA`) -- rejected split-file-into-tmp-dirs approach in favor of in-memory token masking | Discussed whether disabled regions could be implemented by splitting the source file into fragments (written to tmp dirs, cross-platform), formatting each fragment independently, then recombining. Rejected: disabled regions are not guaranteed to align to block boundaries -- a user may disable formatting for one hand-aligned statement mid-block, or accidentally place `ENA` one line early/late relative to nested braces, or intentionally disable from mid-`switch` through the end of an enclosing function. Any of these produces a fragment with unbalanced braces/scope, which several rules cannot tolerate: `ScopePipeline`/`BlockStructureRule` need continuous brace/scope-depth tracking across the whole token stream, and `CppSpecificRule.enforceHeaderFileStructure` (include sort, header guards) and `JavaSpecificRule.enforceImportOrdering` are inherently whole-file and cannot run on a fragment at all. Splitting into files also adds cross-platform tmp-dir/cleanup complexity for no benefit once whole-file rules are considered. **Resolved direction instead:** tokenize the whole file once as normal; tag the token range between each `JXM_CFMT_DIS`/`ENA` marker pair as frozen (on `TokenizerCore.Token` or a parallel range set); every rule treats frozen tokens as opaque pass-through -- skipped for transformation, but still counted for brace/scope-depth/line-number bookkeeping -- so rules needing whole-file context remain correct regardless of where markers fall, including across block boundaries or malformed/off-by-one marker placement. This is a design direction only; which specific rules must be audited to honor the frozen flag, and the exact `Token`-level API, are left to Task A's own implementation/design session. Task F's "add more tests" pass for Task A cannot be written until this mechanism is implemented, since F depends on A's actual marker-handling behavior |
| RDD_KEY_91 | `STATE_KOTLIN.md` — self-contained tracker design, not yet linked from `STATE.md` | Decided in a chat session (not CLI), covering the Kotlin JAR implementation's tracking file before any code exists. Three sub-decisions: (1) Structure — a lighter tracker than `STATE.md`'s full mirror, explicitly self-contained (states up front it must not assume `STATE.md` has been read, restates the commit workflow rather than cross-referencing it) so a future CLI session can pick up Kotlin work without first reading the much larger `STATE.md`/`RDD_LOG.md` history. (2) Linkage — deliberately **not** added to `STATE.md`'s own Project Layout or checklist yet, per explicit user instruction; `STATE.md` remains untouched by this work until told otherwise. (3) Shared log — `STATE_KOTLIN.md` logs its own resolved decisions into this same `RDD_LOG.md` file, continuing the existing `RDD_KEY_n` sequence (not a separate `RDD_KOTLIN_KEY_n` numbering), since it is the same underlying formatter codebase and collision-free continuation is simpler than maintaining two independently-numbered logs — same reasoning already applied to `STATE_NEXT_AI.md`'s `RDD_EXT_n` entries, except here there was no separate-numbering need in the first place since nothing about this decision requires isolating it from the main sequence. |
| RDD_KEY_92 | Shared-tokenizer approach for Kotlin — extend `TokenizerCore.java` in place, no separate Kotlin tokenizer class | Decided in the same chat session as RDD_KEY_91, confirming the existing shared-tokenizer architecture (already used identically by C, C++, and Java — see `Lang` class, `isC`/`isCpp`/`isJava` precedent under Task E) extends the same way for Kotlin: new keyword sets (`KEYWORDS_KOTLIN`), multi-char operators (`?.`, `!!`, `?:`, `..`, `..<`), and named-construct detection are added additively to `TokenizerCore.java` itself, gated by language where needed, rather than introducing a fourth per-language tokenizer class. Rationale: every existing per-language rule class (`JavaSpecificRule`, `CppSpecificRule`) already sits on top of one shared tokenizer; forking the tokenizer itself for Kotlin would break that precedent for no documented benefit, and would require duplicating already-COMPLETE generic-token/comment/string-literal handling that Kotlin needs unchanged. Hard constraint carried forward into `STATE_KOTLIN.md`: every such addition must be additive and verified against the full existing C/C++/Java test suite before any Kotlin-specific rule work begins, since a tokenizer regression would silently corrupt three already-shipping languages, not just fail to help the fourth. |
| RDD_KEY_93 | `STATE_KOTLIN.md` checklist ordering — tokenizer support first, then a `JavaSpecificRule`-style scoping pass, before any `KotlinSpecificRule.java` code | Decided in the same chat session as RDD_KEY_91/92. Two-part resolution, both confirmed by the user: (1) Step 0 (tokenizer) must land and pass the full existing regression suite before Step 1 begins, since every later step's detection logic (named-construct lookback, modifier-priority mapping, rule-class scoping) depends on Kotlin tokens already being lexed correctly — attempting the scoping pass first would mean re-deriving token-shape assumptions that Step 0 would immediately invalidate. (2) The scoping pass itself (Step 1) explicitly mirrors `JavaSpecificRule.java`'s own scoping methodology (RDD_KEY_59) — cross-check each `STYLE_KOTLIN.md`/`STYLE_KOTLIN2.md` section against already-COMPLETE shared classes before writing any `KotlinSpecificRule.java` code, producing a recorded per-section table rather than assuming reuse vs. new-code status ahead of time. This ordering is a direct precedent reuse, not a new invention — `CppSpecificRule.java` and `JavaSpecificRule.java` were both scoped this way before their own implementation checklists were written. |
| RDD_KEY_94 | Comment-grammar classifier — deterministic rules vs. classifier split | Decided in a chat session, scoping the accuracy upgrade to the existing `normalize-comment-start-case`/`normalize-comment-end-period` keys (Task B). Rules own the safe-skip side (block-closing comments, code-shaped syntax, multi-sentence detection, very short comments) with near-zero false-positive risk and need no classifier. The classifier's only job is the narrower ambiguous-but-legitimate prose tail that rules would otherwise conservatively skip. Trailing-comment position is explicitly *not* a reliable skip signal on its own (e.g. `return a + b; // Return the sum` is a full sentence) — trailing comments get the same content-based judgment as standalone comments. |
| RDD_KEY_95 | Comment-grammar classifier — non-Latin script gate is presence-based, not ratio-based | Decided in the same session as RDD_KEY_94. Any non-Latin-script codepoint anywhere in the comment disables the classifier for that comment entirely (equivalent to ABSTAIN); this is a permanent scope boundary, not a tunable threshold. Rationale: a linear classifier with English-shaped features has no representational basis to judge grammar/word order in non-Latin-script languages, and a mixed-token comment (e.g. `// return UNICODE_WORDS`, a Latin keyword embedded in foreign-language prose) defeats whole-string language-ID too, since high-frequency English/code tokens skew detection toward "English." Ratio-based and n-gram whole-string language-ID were both considered and rejected for this reason. Non-Latin comments remain in scope for the existing full-file AI pass, which has genuine language understanding — this classifier does not attempt to replace it. |
| RDD_KEY_96 | Comment-grammar classifier — per-language keyword lists, two-stage ambiguity check | Decided in the same session as RDD_KEY_94/95. No shared keyword/config list across C/C++/Java/Kotlin — each language gets its own reserved-word list, since common English prepositions double as keywords in some languages but not others (e.g. Kotlin's `to`/`in`/`is`/`as`/`by` vs. C/C++/Java). Two-stage check per word: (1) cheap membership test against that language's own keyword list — if not a member, treat as ordinary prose with no further check; (2) only for actual keyword matches, feed contextual features (previous/next word, structural neighbors, position) so the classifier judges code-usage vs. prose-usage for that specific instance. Avoids both a brittle global blocklist and the cost of running full contextual scoring on every token. |
| RDD_KEY_97 | Comment-grammar classifier — weight determination is frontier-model-assisted, not corpus-trained, for v1 | Decided in the same session as RDD_KEY_94–96. v1 weights are generated offline, once, per language, by giving a frontier AI model the feature list, scoring formula, and a representative example set (covering block-closing/trailing/keyword-ambiguous/multi-sentence cases); the resulting static weight vector is baked into the JAR as constants. Fully deterministic at runtime, zero AI dependency in the shipped formatter — same determinism guarantee as hand-tuning, without manually reasoning through feature-interaction weights by hand. Corpus-trained weights (real labeled data + logistic regression) are an explicit future upgrade path, swappable in without any architecture change, since the feature vector and scoring formula are identical either way. Rejected: pure hand-set weights (equal determinism, more manual effort, no clear advantage); full corpus training as v1 (data-collection/infra cost not justified before real coverage data exists). |
| RDD_KEY_98 | Comment-grammar classifier — accuracy/coverage target | Decided in the same session as RDD_KEY_94–97. Target is 99% precision on the positive ("normalize") decision, with the confidence threshold set from real/representative data to hit that precision — coverage is measured as a result, not targeted directly. Matches standard practice for silent auto-apply tools (no per-instance user review), between suggestion-only tools (~90–95%) and irreversible high-stakes systems (99.9%+, likely unachievable with a linear model regardless). False skip (leaving a normalizable comment untouched) is zero-cost; false positive (wrong normalization) is a visible correctness bug — this asymmetry is why ABSTAIN-on-low-confidence is the default behavior, not a fallback edge case. |
| RDD_KEY_99 | Kotlin headless named-construct classification (`companion object {}`, anonymous `object [: Super] {}`, `init {}`) — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §3.1/§3.4 | Step 1's scoping table flagged that these three shapes never arm the tokenizer's `pendingNamedConstructName` (no IDENTIFIER token follows their triggering keyword), so `BlockStructureRule.classifyBrace` fell through to `Frame.other`, losing the mandatory always-blank-lines/always-closing-comment NAMED treatment. Resolved as an additive extension, not a behavior change to any existing shape (mirrors the `isAnonymousClassBrace`/RDD_KEY_84 precedent): new `Lang.isKotlin` field (parallel to `isC`/`isCpp`/`isJava`) computed once in the constructor; new `BlockStructureRule.classifyKotlinHeadlessNamed(tokens, prevIdx)`, called from `classifyBrace` only when `lang.isKotlin` and `brace.name == null` (i.e. never reached for already-correctly-handled named shapes like `object Foo {`/`companion object Foo {}`, which the tokenizer already arms and `classifyNamed` already labels generically). Returns `"init"` for a bare `init` keyword directly before `{`; walks backward from `{` over an optional `: SuperType(...), Iface<T>, ...` clause (reusing existing `matchOpenBackward`/`matchAngleOpenBackward`) to find a bare `object` keyword, checking one more token back for `companion` to distinguish `"companion object"` from plain `"object"`; returns null (falls through to `Frame.other`, unchanged existing behavior) for anything that doesn't match this shape. **Second, deeper bug found and fixed during verification, not anticipated by the scoping table**: a standalone harness (`addClosingComments` on `val x = object : Comparable<Int> { }`) showed `} // Comparable` instead of `} // object` — the tokenizer's `pendingNamedConstructName` arming mechanism (`namedConstructKeywordSeen`, used generically by every language) captures the *first* IDENTIFIER seen after any named-construct keyword regardless of whether a `:` inheritance-clause boundary was crossed first, because C/C++/Java's grammar always has the construct's own name appear *before* any such colon, so this ordering was never exercised before. Kotlin's anonymous `object : Comparable<Int>` is the first case where the keyword is followed directly by `:` with no name at all, so the mechanism wrongly walked into the supertype clause and grabbed `Comparable`. Fixed additively in the shared tokenizer: new branch in `TokenizerCore.trackSignificant` clearing `namedConstructKeywordSeen` when a `:` token is seen while it is still true (i.e., no name has been armed yet) — inert for C/C++/Java since that flag is always already false by the time any of their constructs reaches a `:`. A related bug in my own new `classifyKotlinHeadlessNamed` scan surfaced from the same root confusion: `:` tokenizes as an `OP` token (falls through `emitOperator`'s final single-char fallback, never reaches `emitPunct`), not `PUNCT` — both the tokenizer fix and the backward-scan's own `:` check had to use `isOp(t, ":")`, not `isPunct(t, ":")`, which was the first (wrong) version of each and silently matched nothing until corrected. Verified via a standalone harness (not committed, scratchpad only): `companion object {}` (anonymous, no supertype) → `// companion object`; anonymous `object : Comparable<Int> {}` → `// object`; anonymous `object : Base(), Iface {}` (multiple supertypes, one with constructor-call parens) → `// object`; bare `object {}` (no supertype at all) → `// object`; `companion object : Base() {}` → `// companion object`; `init {}` → `// init`; and the pre-existing named `companion object Named {}` shape confirmed unaffected (still handled by the generic already-COMPLETE `classifyNamed` path, labeled `// Named` — a known, separate, out-of-scope label-precision gap, not this item's concern). Full C/C++/Java suite re-run 25/25 both before and after every change in this item (three separate shared-class edits: `Lang.java`, `BlockStructureRule.java`, `TokenizerCore.java`). |
| RDD_KEY_100 | Kotlin `when` no-space-before-`(` — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §3.2 | Step 1's scoping table flagged this as "(b)": `MiscRule.TIGHT_PAREN_KEYWORDS` (the set backing `enforceKeywordSpacing`, STYLE.md §3.2's `if`/`while`/`for`/`switch`/`catch` no-space-before-`(` rule) isn't language-partitioned at all — it's a single shared `Set<String>` checked purely by keyword text, with no per-language gating anywhere in the method. Adding `"when"` to it is therefore a pure additive no-op for C/C++/Java: they have no `when` keyword/token (not in `KEYWORDS_C`/`KEYWORDS_CPP`/`KEYWORDS_JAVA`), so the set membership check can never match for those languages regardless of what's added. Verified via a standalone harness (not committed, scratchpad only): tokenizing Kotlin source `when (x) { 1 -> "a" }` and calling `enforceKeywordSpacing` directly produced `when(x) { 1 -> "a" }` — space collapsed correctly. Full C/C++/Java suite 25/25 both before and after (single one-line, one-set-element change to a shared class). |
| RDD_KEY_101 | Kotlin `when` expression arrow alignment, closing comment, blank lines — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §4 | Structurally the analog of Java's arrow-form `switch` expression (STYLE_JAVA17.md §3.1), but reusing Java's implementation turned out not to be possible. First, `SwitchRule.java` (read in full, 896 lines) is entirely colon-form switch STATEMENT handling (STYLE.md §13 — `SwitchBlock`/`CaseLabel` discovery via literal `"switch"`/`"case"`/`"default"` keyword and `:` colon detection, inline/non-inline blank-line and indent normalization, fallthrough marking) with zero `"->"` handling at all — a correction to the Step 1 scoping table's framing, which had implied this was the file to read. The actual arrow-form logic lives in `JavaSpecificRule.enforceSwitchExpressionArrowAlignment`/`findArrowCases` (read in full, lines ~940-1139), and two of its mechanisms don't generalize to Kotlin: (1) `findArrowCases` anchors every label's start by scanning for a `case`/`default` KEYWORD token, but Kotlin `when` branches have no such leading keyword — just a bare condition expression (or `else`) directly followed by `->`; (2) `enforceSwitchExpressionArrowAlignment` bails out of alignment for the *entire* switch if any one case has a block body (`anyBlockBody` check, all-or-nothing), but STYLE_KOTLIN.md §4's own worked example explicitly keeps `->` column-aligned even with one block-body branch present (`2    -> {` aligned alongside `1    -> foo()`/`else -> qux()`) — the opposite rule. Implemented instead as a new, fully independent `KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions(List<Token>)`: finds every `when [(subject)] { ... }` (subject-less `when { ... }` supported too, labeled `// when` with no subject text), then locates each branch via `findWhenBranches` under a one-branch-per-physical-line requirement (the only way to unambiguously locate a branch boundary without a leading keyword to anchor on, and the shape every STYLE_KOTLIN.md §4 example already uses): a depth-0 (relative to nested parens/brackets/braces) `->` starts a branch's body; the boundary to the next branch's label is either that body's own matching `}` (block body) or the next depth-0 NEWLINE (non-block body). If this shape doesn't hold for a given `when` (`findWhenBranches` returns null), that `when` is left completely untouched — same conservative "never guess past an unrecognized shape" posture used throughout `SwitchRule.java`/`JavaSpecificRule.java`. Every branch is then aligned independently (no all-or-nothing bailout), padding each label via `ColumnGrid` and rewriting only the label+arrow span, skipping (per-branch, not per-`when`) any row whose padded label would push its own same-physical-line body content past `lineLengthLimit` — same predict-before-committing idempotency guard as `JavaSpecificRule.applyArrowAlignment`. Blank lines are forced (not merely preserved) after the opening `{` and before the closing `}`, unlike `BlockStructureRule.insertNamedConstructBlankLines`'s explicit "control-flow blocks' existing blank lines are preserved exactly as written, never forced" behavior for `if`/`for`/`while`/`switch` — a second point of required divergence from any existing `Kind`'s treatment, resolved by writing a small self-contained `ensureBlankLineInGap` in `KotlinSpecificRule` itself (conservative: bails on that one gap, not the whole `when`, if a comment sits inside it, since relocating a comment relative to an inserted blank line isn't handled). The closing comment (`// when subject`) is unconditional regardless of body length, unlike FOR/WHILE/SWITCH's length-gated closing comments in `BlockStructureRule.addClosingComments` (`closingCommentMinLines`) — matching NAMED-construct treatment and Java's own switch-EXPRESSION closing-comment precedent (an expression, not a statement, so no length gate applies). Verified via a standalone harness (not committed, scratchpad only) covering three cases: a simple `when(x) { 1 -> foo() ... }`, the exact mixed simple/block-body example from STYLE_KOTLIN.md §4 itself, and a subject-less `when { a -> foo() ... }` — all three produced output matching the style doc's exact expected rendering, including the block-body-mixed alignment case that Java's rule would have refused to align at all. This method is new standalone code in a not-yet-wired-in class (`KotlinSpecificRule` isn't called from `Formatter.formatOne` yet, per Step 3's own scope), so the full C/C++/Java suite (25/25 before and after) only confirms no accidental shared-class regression, not end-to-end Kotlin wiring — that remains for whenever `KotlinSpecificRule` itself gets wired in. |
| RDD_KEY_102 | Kotlin null-safety operator spacing (`?.`/`!!` tight, `?:` spaced) — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §5 | Step 1's scoping table flagged this as "(c)": no shared class does general expression-level operator re-spacing today — `MiscRule.isTightToken`/`needsSpaceBetween` only fire inside signature/param rendering, and assignment RHS values are joined verbatim (`MiscRule.joinVerbatim`, no re-spacing at all) — so, unlike §3.x/§4, this couldn't be scoped to one construct at all: `?.`, `!!`, and `?:` can appear in any expression anywhere in a Kotlin file. Confirmed the tokenizer already emits all three as single multi-char OP tokens (`TokenizerCore.MULTI_CHAR_OPS`), so no tokenizer change was needed. Implemented as new `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceNullSafetyOperatorSpacing(List<Token>)`: a single flat pass over the whole token stream (no bracket-depth tracking needed, unlike §4, since this only ever inspects the immediate gap on either side of one operator token at a time) that, for the gap between every pair of adjacent significant tokens, collapses it to nothing if either side is `?.`/`!!`, normalizes it to exactly one space if either side is `?:`, and otherwise leaves it completely untouched (in particular, a plain `.`'s own spacing is never touched by this method either way, since that's a separate, still out-of-scope concern — confirmed during verification, see below). Same conservative bailout used throughout the codebase's other whitespace-collapsing passes: any gap containing a comment, a NEWLINE, or a frozen token is left exactly as-is, so a null-safety operator split across physical lines, or one sitting next to a disabled/frozen span, is never touched. Verified via a standalone harness (not committed, scratchpad only) with five cases: `str ?. length ?: 0` → `str?.length ?: 0` (both operators fixed independently in one pass); `user !! . name` → `user!!. name` (the `!!` correctly pulled tight against its neighbors, while the pre-existing space between the unrelated `.` and `name` was correctly left alone — this was checked deliberately, since it's the one case that could look like a missed spot but is actually correct scoping, not a bug: general `.` spacing is out of scope everywhere else in this codebase too); `a?.b?.c!!.d ?: e` (already fully correct) staying byte-identical; a `?:` split across a newline left untouched; and a `?.` immediately followed by a trailing `//` comment left untouched. Full C/C++/Java suite 25/25 (new method, no shared-class change, and not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` — same "verified standalone, not end-to-end" caveat as §4/RDD_KEY_101). |
| RDD_KEY_103 | Kotlin variable/property declaration alignment — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §6 | Step 1 had flagged this "(c) — major, see Open Questions": `DeclarationAlignmentRule.Declaration` and its `parseDeclaration`/`render` assume C/Java's `[modifiers] Type name [= init]` token order, while Kotlin's actual grammar is `[modifiers] val/var name [: type] [= init]` — name before type, reversed. The Open Questions section had originally resolved this (for both §6 and §7) as "give `KotlinSpecificRule.java` its own fully independent parser/renderer, don't touch `DeclarationAlignmentRule`". Mid-session the user reconsidered specifically for §6 and asked to evaluate subclassing `DeclarationAlignmentRule` instead. Investigation found nearly every Kotlin-relevant internal of `DeclarationAlignmentRule` is `private` (only the constructors, `groupDeclarations`, `reorderStatics`, and `render` are non-private) — so a subclass would reuse almost nothing without a visibility change first. Posed this tradeoff back to the user via a direct question; the user chose "loosen shared-class visibility, then extend" over "separate file, no inheritance" (which had been offered as the recommended option). Implemented in two parts. (1) In `DeclarationAlignmentRule.java`, raised six private helpers to `protected` — `splitStatements`, `hasBlankLineBefore`, `hasCommentBefore`, `significantOnly`, `renderTokens`, `findTrailingComment` — each confirmed fully C/C++/Java-grammar-agnostic (no reference to `Declaration`'s type-before-name assumptions), so this is purely an access-modifier change with zero behavior difference; confirmed via the full C/C++/Java suite staying 25/25 both before and after. `parseDeclaration`, `renderInitTokens`, and `render` itself were deliberately NOT touched/reused — they're irreducibly C/C++/Java-specific (bitfields, structured bindings, function-pointer detection, C-style-cast/pointer-deref disambiguation, hardcoded type-before-name column order). (2) New file `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule extends DeclarationAlignmentRule`, package `com.jxmake.formatter.rules`, reusing those six promoted helpers plus the already-public `ColumnGrid`/`ModifierPriority`/`KotlinModifierPriority` and `Declaration`'s own package-private constructor accessibility (unchanged, no edit needed there). Introduces its own `KotlinDecl` record (`modifiers` list including the `val`/`var` keyword itself, `name`, `typeTokens`, `initTokens`, `trailingComment`). Discovered during implementation (not anticipated in the original design sketch) that the inherited `splitStatements` could not actually be reused for finding statement *boundaries*, only for its other helpers: it only ever splits on a top-level `;` or a `}` that closes a brace-initializer, both because C/C++/Java always terminate a declaration with `;` — but idiomatic Kotlin properties have no `;` at all and are newline-terminated, so reusing `splitStatements` verbatim silently merged an entire scope's worth of consecutive `val`/`var` lines into one giant unparseable "statement" (caught by a harness test, not by inspection — the first harness run against the STYLE_KOTLIN.md §6 worked examples produced one merged blob per test instead of per-line groups). Fixed by writing a parallel `splitKotlinStatements` in the new subclass: same paren/bracket/brace depth tracking as the base method, but splits at a depth-0 NEWLINE token whenever at least one significant token has been seen since the last split (in addition to still splitting on `;`, since Kotlin permits but doesn't require it) — one declaration per physical line, matching every STYLE_KOTLIN.md §6 worked example; a declaration whose initializer genuinely spans multiple physical lines will get cut mid-expression and `parseKotlinDeclaration` will simply fail to parse the truncated result (leftover unconsumed tokens), so that entry is conservatively left out of any alignment group rather than mis-aligned — no worked example in the style doc exercises this, so it's an accepted, deliberate limitation rather than a bug. `parseKotlinDeclaration` walks a statement's significant tokens directly as `[modifiers] val|var name [: type] [= init]`, returning null (which breaks the current alignment group, same posture as the base class's unrecognized-declaration handling) for any statement that doesn't match — a function call, control-flow statement, function/class declaration, or an annotation-prefixed property (annotations aren't recognized as modifiers in this first pass) all correctly fall through to null. `renderPropertyGroup` builds a `ColumnGrid` with one column per field — a variable number of modifier-rank columns (from `KotlinModifierPriority.columnCount()`, only the ranks actually used by *some* row in the group are added as columns, mirroring the base class's `modifierActive`-conditional-column precedent), then name, then `": " + type` (added only if some row in the group has an explicit type), then `"= " + init` (added only if some row has an initializer), then trailing comment (added only if present) — letting `ColumnGrid.flush()`'s automatic per-column padding produce the `:`-column-detached-from-name and `=`-column alignment shown in the style doc's worked examples directly, without the manual width pre-computation the base class's `render()` needs only for its bitfield/aggregate-init edge cases (which don't exist in Kotlin's simpler grammar). A row's own genuinely-absent trailing field renders as an empty string; since `ColumnGrid.flush()` never pads a row's own last cell (rows may have different lengths), and joining an empty last cell with `" "` still leaves one stray trailing space, `renderPropertyGroup` runs the joined line through a small trailing-whitespace trim before returning it. Also found and fixed, during verification against the `= str?.length ?: 0` worked example, a cross-feature integration gap between this new subclass and §5/RDD_KEY_102: the inherited `renderTokens` uses the base class's own `isTightToken` table, which has no notion of Kotlin's `?.`/`!!` tight null-safety operators (that recognition lives entirely in the separate whole-file `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceNullSafetyOperatorSpacing` pass, which isn't and shouldn't be wired into this grid-cell-level renderer) — so `renderTokens` was leaving one stray space on either side of `?.`/`!!` inside a rendered type or init cell. Fixed narrowly and locally (not by modifying the shared `isTightToken`) with a small `renderKotlinTokens` wrapper around `renderTokens` that regex-strips exactly that leftover space around `?.`/`!!` in the returned string; `?:`'s existing single-space-both-sides default from the base class's generic spacing rule needed no fix, since it already matched STYLE_KOTLIN.md §5's spacing requirement by coincidence of `isTightToken` not listing it as tight. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed) covering: the exact STYLE_KOTLIN.md §6 visibility/modality/override worked example (aligned `:` column, several inactive-but-used modifier columns rendering as blank space of consistent width); the exact `val`/`=`-alignment worked example including a `?.`/`!!`/`?:`-bearing initializer; a group broken mid-way by a blank line; a mix of declarations with and without an explicit type/initializer in the same group (confirming column-index consistency isn't broken by the conditional-column logic); and a non-declaration statement (a bare function call) correctly breaking the group rather than being silently absorbed or crashing the parser. All five matched the exact expected output byte-for-byte. Full C/C++/Java suite 25/25 both after the visibility-only base-class edit and after the new subclass file was added (new file, no shared-class behavior change; not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne`, same "verified standalone, not end-to-end" caveat as every other Step 3 item so far). |
| RDD_KEY_104 | Kotlin constructor/function parameter list line-breaking and column alignment — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §7/§7.1 | Step 1 had flagged this "(c)", tied to §6's resolution: `MiscRule.Param`/`Signature` and its `parseSignature`/`render` assume C/Java's `[typeTokens] name [sizeTokens]` token order, while Kotlin's actual grammar is `[modifiers] name : type [= default]` -- name before type, reversed, same class of issue as §6/RDD_KEY_103 but in a different shared class. Posed the same tradeoff back to the user directly (extend `MiscRule` via visibility-loosening, mirroring RDD_KEY_103, vs. an independent parser in `KotlinSpecificRule.java`); the user chose the same answer as §6: loosen shared-class visibility, then extend. Implemented in two parts. (1) In `MiscRule.java`, raised six private helpers to `protected` -- `matchParenForward`, `significantOnly`, `significantWithComments`, `splitTopLevelCommas`, `renderTokens`, `indentText` -- each confirmed fully C/C++/Java-grammar-agnostic (no reference to `Param`/`Signature`'s type-before-name assumptions), so this is purely an access-modifier change with zero behavior difference; confirmed via the full C/C++/Java suite staying 32/32 both before and after. `parseSignature`, `parseParam`, and both `render` overloads were deliberately NOT touched/reused -- irreducibly C/C++/Java-specific (type-before-name parsing, C array-param `[size]` suffix peeling, the manual `maxTypeLen`/`maxNameCommaLen` width pre-computation §8's own worked example requires). (2) New file `KotlinSignatureRule extends MiscRule`, reusing those six promoted helpers plus the already-public `ColumnGrid`. Introduces `KotlinParam` (`modifiers`, `name`, `typeTokens` -- never empty on a successful parse, since every Kotlin function/constructor param requires an explicit type, unlike a §6 property's optional inferred type -- `defaultTokens`, comments) and `KotlinSignature` (`leadTokens`, `name`, `params`, `trailingComma`) models. `parseKotlinSignature` duplicates `parseSignature`'s small name/open-paren boundary-finding loop rather than extracting it as a shared method (same "exact copy, not shared utility" precedent already established for this file's own `renderTokens` lineage -- the loop is ~15 lines and isn't type-order-dependent either way, so sharing it would save little). `parseKotlinParam` walks `[modifiers] name : type [= default]` directly, requiring the `:` and a non-empty type, returning null (bailing the whole signature, matching `MiscRule.parseParam`'s own posture for an unrecognized shape) otherwise. Trailing-comma preservation (§7.2, "preserve exactly as written", extended from just enum entries per §2/RDD_KEY_99's precedent to every parameter-list context per the style doc's own text): `splitTopLevelCommas` leaves an empty final part when the source's last param is itself followed by a comma before `)` -- captured as `KotlinSignature.trailingComma` before being dropped from the parsed param list, then re-emitted (or not) on render, both inline and broken. `render` reuses the same fits-inline-else-break-one-per-line line-length decision as `MiscRule.render(Signature, ...)`, but the broken form uses a `ColumnGrid` (name, `: type`, `= default`) rather than the base class's manual width pre-computation -- same "grammar simple enough that the grid alone produces the alignment" reasoning as `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderPropertyGroup`/RDD_KEY_103. **One real bug caught and fixed during verification, not anticipated in the original design**: the trailing comma was initially modeled as its own grid column, which made `ColumnGrid` pad every row's `: type` cell out to its widest sibling *before* the comma (`val id    : Long   ,` -- three stray spaces before the comma), since `ColumnGrid.flush()` only ever leaves a row's own true *last* cell unpadded, and a bare `,` cell was consuming that "last cell" slot instead of the type text itself. Fixed by attaching the comma directly onto whichever cell is actually semantically last for that row (the type cell if no default, the default cell if one is present) instead of giving it a column of its own -- verified this now exactly matches STYLE_KOTLIN.md §7's own worked example (`val id    : Long,`, no gap before the comma). Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): the exact §7 short/inline and long/broken `data class Point`/`User` worked examples (byte-for-byte match, including the fixed comma spacing); a broken signature with a trailing comma preserved (both inline and broken forms); a `fun foo(x: Int = 10, y: Int = 20)` inline default-value case (§7.1); a broken signature where every parameter has a default value, confirming the `:`/`=` columns both align correctly together (§7.1 "same as §6"); and a `vararg` modifier-bearing parameter parsing and rendering correctly inline. **Not covered**: call-site named arguments (`foo(x = 1, y = 2)`), a structurally different, type-less shape (function-call argument, not a parameter declaration) outside `KotlinSignature`'s scope as parsed here -- would need its own small parser/renderer analogous to `MiscRule`'s `renderCallOnePerLine`/`renderCallPreserveGroups` family if picked up later. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 (new file, no shared-class behavior change; not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` or covered by a `test/kt_*` fixture, both deferred to Step 4/5, same "verified standalone, not end-to-end" caveat as every other Step 3 item so far). |
| RDD_KEY_105 | Kotlin labeled jump / label declaration spacing (`return@label`/`break@loop`/`continue@loop`, `label@`) — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §11 | Step 1 had flagged this "(c)": no existing mechanism recognizes this token shape at all -- a keyword or identifier immediately glued to `@` -- since it has no analog anywhere in C/C++/Java. Confirmed the tokenizer already emits `@` as its own single-char OP token via the generic operator fallback, so no tokenizer change was needed, same as §5/RDD_KEY_102's `?.`/`!!`/`?:`. Implemented as a new, fully self-contained `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceLabeledJumpSpacing(List<Token>)`: a single flat pass over the whole token stream (same shape as `enforceNullSafetyOperatorSpacing`), driven by a small left-to-right `JumpState` enum (NONE, AFTER_JUMP_KEYWORD, AFTER_JUMP_AT, AFTER_JUMP_LABEL, AFTER_PLAIN_IDENT, AFTER_DECL_AT) rather than a fixed lastSignificant-only lookback, since telling a jump's `@label` apart from a declaration's `label@` requires remembering one token further back than a single-gap check alone can see. Classification: `return`/`break`/`continue` (KEYWORD) immediately followed by `@` is a jump -- the gap before that `@` is collapsed to nothing, and the gap between the `@` and the following label IDENTIFIER is also collapsed to nothing, giving `return@forEach`. A bare IDENTIFIER immediately followed by `@` is instead treated as a label declaration (Kotlin's grammar has no other valid construct where an identifier is glued directly to `@` -- annotations always put the `@` first, never after an identifier) -- the gap before that `@` is collapsed to nothing, giving `outer@`. Whatever comes right after a jump's label identifier, or right after a declaration's `@`, is forced to exactly one space if anything follows on the same physical line (`return@label value`, `outer@ for(...)`) -- both cases share one "force space" branch in the state machine, since STYLE_KOTLIN.md §11's own text describes both as "spaced from what follows" the same way a normal keyword is spaced from a following identifier. Any `@` not matching either predecessor pattern (an `@Override`-style annotation, or a §16 use-site target like `@field:`) leaves `JumpState` at NONE and is left completely untouched, correctly staying out of this method's scope. Same conservative bailout as §5: a gap containing a comment, a NEWLINE, or a frozen token is left entirely as-is -- in particular, this is what correctly prevents a bare `return@label` with nothing following it on the same line (the next token being the statement's own closing NEWLINE) from having a stray trailing space forced onto it. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed) with six cases, several deliberately mis-spaced in the input to confirm the pass actively normalizes rather than merely leaving already-correct input untouched: `return @ forEach` -> `return@forEach` (inside the exact §11 `forEach`/`if` worked example, including the blank line after it being left alone since blank-line handling is out of this method's scope); `outer @  for(...) ... break  @  outer` -> `outer@ for(...) ... break@outer` (the exact §11 nested-loop worked example, both the label declaration and the jump normalized independently in one pass); a bare `continue@loop` with nothing following on its line, confirming no trailing space was wrongly forced before the closing NEWLINE; `return@label value` and its mis-spaced form `return @ label   value`, both collapsing to the identical correct output; and `outer@for(...)` (already-tight, no space at all before `for`) correctly gaining the required one space, confirming the pass forces the space even when the source has none, not just when preserving an existing one. All six matched STYLE_KOTLIN.md §11's expected spacing exactly. This method needed no `MiscRule`/`DeclarationAlignmentRule` visibility changes at all (unlike RDD_KEY_103/RDD_KEY_104) since it's fully self-contained within `KotlinSpecificRule` and doesn't reuse or extend any C/C++/Java-side shared class. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 (new method, no shared-class change whatsoever; not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` or covered by a `test/kt_*` fixture, same "verified standalone, not end-to-end" caveat as every other Step 3 item so far). |
| RDD_KEY_106 | Kotlin generic `where` clause line-breaking and bound alignment — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §14 | Step 1 had flagged this "(c)": a structural analog exists in `CppSpecificRule.enforceRequiresClausePlacement` (C++20 trailing `requires` clause), but that's itself a per-language file, not shared, so it needed its own `KotlinSpecificRule` method rather than a shared-class extension — used the C++ method as a reference pattern per the Step 1 table's own suggestion. Implemented as new `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceWhereClausePlacement(List<Token>)`, structurally mirroring the C++ method's decision logic (fits inline vs. wraps to its own line based on `lineLengthLimit`) but differing in one respect the style doc requires and C++'s single-clause `requires` doesn't need: STYLE_KOTLIN.md §14's own worked example always breaks every bound onto its own line, one per top-level comma, column-aligned under the first bound's start column, whenever the clause doesn't fit inline as a whole — there's no intermediate "wrap `where` but keep all bounds on one line" state. Bound splitting (`splitWhereBounds`) tracks depth over parens/brackets/braces and the tokenizer's own reclassified `ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`/`_CLOSE` tokens (confirmed via `TokenizerCore.reclassifyAngleBrackets`, already generic and Kotlin-safe) so a bound's own generic argument (`Comparable<T>`) is never mistaken for a bound separator. Bound text itself is never rewritten (per the style doc's explicit "the bound's own `:`... stays glued to its type parameter", read as "don't break within a bound", not a spacing instruction) — only `where`'s placement and the inter-bound commas/newlines are touched, via an overrides+insertAfter+suppressed-index render (added a `renderSuppressing` variant of this file's existing `render` to let a bound's already-correct interior gap tokens be elided once the bound's start index carries the whole replacement text). A single bound line that still doesn't fit is left to overflow untouched, per the style doc's own explicit exception (same posture as §12's destructuring lists) — no further break point exists below one-bound-per-line. Added a new `KotlinSpecificRule(Lang, int lineLengthLimit, int indentWidth)` constructor (mirroring `CppSpecificRule`'s own `indentUnit` precedent) since this is the first Kotlin method needing the configured indent width rather than just line length. **One bug caught during verification, not anticipated in the original design**: the initial implementation only replaced the `where` keyword token itself with `"\n" + indent + "where"`, leaving the original single-space gap between the signature's return type and `where` untouched — this produced a stray trailing space at the end of the signature's own line (`"...): T \n    where..."`). Fixed by also suppressing every gap token immediately preceding `where` back to the previous significant token. A second issue found in the same pass: the initial comment-detection check only scanned each bound's own token span, missing a comment sitting in the gap *between* two bounds (e.g. right after a comma) — replaced with a `hasCommentBetween` scan (same precedent/name as `CppSpecificRule`'s own helper) covering the clause's entire span between `where` and its terminating `{`/`;`, so any comment anywhere in the clause now correctly blocks the rewrite. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed) covering: the exact §14 "fits inline" and "doesn't fit" worked examples (byte-for-byte match, including the 10-space continuation-bound indent computed as `indentUnit.length() + "where ".length()`); the §14 "single bound line still doesn't fit" overflow case (left to overflow, no further breaking); a comment placed between two bounds (correctly bails out, clause left completely untouched); and a class-level `where` clause (`class Box<T> where T : ..., T : ... {`), confirming the method isn't accidentally scoped to function signatures only, matching the style doc's general "trailing qualifier on the signature" framing rather than a function-specific one. `where` is already a `KEYWORDS_KOTLIN` entry and needed no tokenizer change. This method needed no `MiscRule`/`DeclarationAlignmentRule`/`BlockStructureRule` visibility changes or extensions at all — fully self-contained within `KotlinSpecificRule`, same posture as RDD_KEY_105. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 (new method + new constructor overload, no shared-class change whatsoever; not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` or covered by a `test/kt_*` fixture, same "verified standalone, not end-to-end" caveat as every other Step 3 item so far). |
| RDD_KEY_107 | Kotlin destructuring declarations — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §12 | Step 1 had flagged this "(c)": the LHS is a parenthesized name list (`(a, b) = pair`), not `MiscRule.Assignment`'s single-`target`-token assumption nor `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`/RDD_KEY_103's `[modifiers] val|var name [: type] [= init]` shape (name is a single IDENTIFIER there, not a parenthesized list) — needed its own parsing, though the table's own note suggested it could likely still feed the existing `=`-alignment renderer once parsed. Implemented directly in `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.java` (not a new file) since it already carries every piece of shared infrastructure needed — `splitKotlinStatements`, `hasBlankLineBefore`/`hasCommentBefore`, `significantOnly`, `findTrailingComment`, `isPlainModifier`/`isValOrVar` — and §6/§12 are adjacent, closely related declaration-alignment concerns in the style doc itself. Added a new `DestructuringDecl` model carrying a single pre-rendered `lhsText` cell (`"val (a, b)"`, comma-normalized) rather than a per-component token list — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §12 is explicit that "a destructuring list has no type annotations to anchor a column grid," so (unlike `KotlinDecl`'s multi-column §6 grid) there is no finer-grained column to split out, making this structurally identical in shape to `MiscRule.Assignment`'s own single-`lhsText`-cell + `= value` two-column render, not `KotlinDecl`'s per-field grid. New `groupDestructuringDeclarations`/`parseDestructuringDeclaration`/`renderDestructuringGroup`, deliberately kept as a separate group stream from `groupPropertyDeclarations`/§6 rather than merged into it — a destructuring row's `(a, b)` LHS and a plain declaration's bare name have no shared column shape to align together, and no worked example shows the two mixed in one group; `parseDestructuringDeclaration` requiring a `(` immediately after `val`/`var` (returning null otherwise, same "unrecognized shape breaks the group" posture as every parser in this class) is what keeps a plain single-name declaration from ever being misabsorbed into a destructuring group, verified explicitly (see below). Comma spacing inside the parenthesized list (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §12: "matches function parameter lists — space after, none before") needed no separate whitespace-collapsing pass, unlike §5/§11's operator-spacing methods — since `lhsText` is built fresh token-by-token from the parsed `Component` list (`", "`-joined), any pre-existing comma spacing in the source (correct, absent, or wrong) is irrelevant and always normalized for free as a side effect of the render, matching the Step 1 scoping table's own "no new code needed" framing but for a different mechanical reason (rebuild-from-parse rather than a passive default). Each `Component` supports an optional per-component `: type` (Kotlin's grammar allows one, even though no STYLE_KOTLIN.md §12 worked example shows it) using the same tight-before-colon-one-space-after convention as §6/RDD_KEY_103's own property `: type` rendering. A destructuring declaration with fewer than two components (`val (a) = x`) is left unrecognized (returns null) — no worked example exists to justify guessing whether/how a single-component form should render differently from `KotlinDecl`'s plain single-name shape. Modifiers are preserved in source order rather than reordered via `KotlinModifierPriority` (unlike `renderPropertyGroup`'s per-column grid) since this method has no multi-column layout for them to occupy and no worked example shows a modifier-bearing destructuring declaration to justify picking a canonical order. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): the exact §12 `(a, b)`/`(id, name, _)` worked example (byte-for-byte, including the `_` placeholder handled with zero special-case code, exactly as the style doc's own text predicts); the same input with commas mis-spaced or padded with extra spaces inside the parens (`( id , name , _ )`), confirming the rebuild-from-parse comma normalization actually fires rather than passively preserving already-correct input; a blank line between two destructuring declarations correctly breaking them into two separate one-row groups (each trivially self-aligned); and a plain single-name `val x = 5` statement immediately following a destructuring declaration correctly breaking the group rather than being absorbed or crashing the parser (2 statements → 2 separate one-row groups, not one two-row group). All matched expected output exactly. No shared-class (`MiscRule`/`DeclarationAlignmentRule`/`BlockStructureRule`) change at all — fully additive within the already-Kotlin-only `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 (not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` or covered by a `test/kt_*` fixture, same "verified standalone, not end-to-end" caveat as every other Step 3 item so far). **REVISED under RDD_KEY_126 (user request, post-RDD_KEY_123's discovery of the `kt_combined_out.kt` cross-group alignment expectation):** the "own group stream, never merged with §6's" half of this decision is **superseded** — see RDD_KEY_126 below. Precedent cited: this codebase's own C++ structured-bindings support already aligns `auto [b, c] = somePair;` in the same group as a preceding `int a = xxx;`, so a Kotlin destructuring declaration doing the same is consistent with existing behavior for the analogous C++ shape, not a new exception. Every other part of this entry (the parsing logic, comma-normalization, single-component/no-worked-example exclusions, modifier-order posture) is unaffected by the revision and remains accurate. |
| RDD_KEY_108 | Kotlin annotation use-site target spacing — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §16 | New `enforceAnnotationUseSiteTargetSpacing` in `KotlinSpecificRule.java`, a flat whole-file token-stream pass structurally identical to `enforceLabeledJumpSpacing`/§11/RDD_KEY_105 (small left-to-right state machine over `@` → target → `:` → name, same conservative gap-blocked-by-comment/NEWLINE/frozen bailout, same "collapse the gap entirely" vs. "leave the gap exactly as found" binary per transition — no partial-normalize case exists here since STYLE_KOTLIN.md §16 only specifies tight-both-sides around the `:`, nothing about the `@`-to-target gap or the annotation-name-onward gap). New `USE_SITE_TARGETS` set (`file, property, field, get, set, receiver, param, setparam, delegate`) matched against raw token *text*, not `TokenType.KEYWORD` — checked `TokenizerCore.KEYWORDS_KOTLIN` and found only `field, file, get, param, property, receiver, set, setparam` are lexed as keywords; `delegate` is not (Kotlin soft keyword, contextual only in this position), so matching by text avoids a tokenizer change entirely (this feature required zero shared-class/tokenizer touch, same posture as every other file in this class). Deliberately did *not* enforce spacing on either side of `@` itself (the gap between `@` and the target keyword) — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §16's own text only calls out the `:` as tight, and no rule anywhere in this codebase actively reformats plain annotation spacing either (e.g. `@Override` is never actively tightened if written with a stray space) — enforcing `@`-to-target tightness here would be inventing a rule with no textual backing and no precedent, so left as a passive default matching the rest of the codebase's annotation posture. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): already-tight input left byte-for-byte identical; mis-spaced `@field : JvmField` correctly tightened both sides to `@field:JvmField`; all of `@get:`, `@param:`, `@delegate:` (the non-keyword-lexed one, confirming the text-match approach actually works, not just the keyword-lexed ones) correctly tightened; a plain `@Override` with no colon left completely untouched (falls out of the state machine at the very first "next token isn't a known target" transition, never risks misfiring on an ordinary annotation); an unrelated `:` in a function parameter list (`fun y(z : Int)`) left untouched, confirming the state machine doesn't false-positive on every bare `:` in the file; and a comment interposed between the target and its `:` (`@field /* c */ : JvmField`) correctly blocking the rewrite on that specific gap (left as `@field /* c */ :JvmField` — the *other* gap, after the colon, still tightens independently, same per-gap-independent evaluation as every other pass in this file, not a bug).
