| RDD_KEY_131 | `test/kt_comments_inp.kt`/`kt_comments_out.kt` — fixed all four bugs blocking this fixture (STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 4's last remaining item): (1) missing blank line before a `when` branch led by its own standalone comment, (2) missing blank line before a `return` directly preceded by its own standalone leading comment, (3) a leading blank line inside a function/lambda body not being stripped when its first statement isn't a declaration, (4) the outermost `class Widget` missing its closing blank line + `} // class Widget` comment | **Bug 1** (`KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions`, §4/RDD_KEY_101): `ensureBlankLineInGap` bailed out entirely whenever *any* comment token appeared anywhere in the scanned gap -- but `findWhenBranches`' `labelStart` is a branch's first *significant* token (`nextSignificantIndex` skips comments), so a comment-led branch's own comment ends up inside the gap being scanned even though it visually belongs to the branch, not to the `when`'s own opening brace -- silently disabling the blank-line force for exactly the comment-led-first-branch case. Fixed by replacing the whole-gap comment check with `SwitchRule.ensureBlankLineInGap`'s own comment-anchored mechanism (duplicated here, not shared infrastructure): a standalone comment that starts its own line becomes the scan boundary, the blank line is still guaranteed in the sub-gap strictly before it, and the comment itself is never relocated. (User later hand-edited `kt_comments_out.kt` to drop an analogous blank line the coordinator had originally proposed between the *second* when-branch's own leading comment and the first branch's body -- confirmed only the *first* branch's own leading-comment gap gets this treatment, not every subsequent branch, so no additional per-branch-gap forcing loop was added beyond the pre-existing two call sites (before first branch, after last body).) **Bug 2** (`MiscRule.insertBlankLineBeforeReturn`, STYLE.md §9, shared class): the existing `!hasComment` gate skipped forcing a blank line before `return` entirely whenever any comment sat in the gap, with a comment left in the code that "neither shape has a STYLE.md worked example to justify guessing where the blank line... should go." This fixture supplies exactly that worked example, but checked the equivalent Java case first (`test/java_comments_inp.java`'s own pre-existing `// Line comment before return` / `// return value is z` / `return;` shape) and confirmed via its already-accepted reference output that Java wants the *opposite* behavior (no blank line inserted at all) -- so this is a genuine per-language style difference, not a shared-class gap to close uniformly. Added a Kotlin-only (`lang.isKotlin`) branch: when a comment is present, force the blank line into the sub-gap strictly *after* the last comment in the gap (between the comment and the `return` itself), leaving any existing blank line before the comment untouched -- new `appendGapWithForcedBlankAfterLastComment`, sibling to the existing `appendGapWithForcedBlank`. Java/C++ paths are byte-for-byte unchanged (confirmed via `make test`). **Bug 3**: root-caused by testing every candidate distinguishing signal against the fixture directly (nesting depth, presence of a further-nested `{`, control-flow-shaped first statement) via minimal standalone repros for each -- all reproduced "blank line always kept, never actually stripped by anything today" in isolation, ruling out a state/ordering bug and confirming this needed genuinely new code, not a fix to existing broken logic. The one signal that held up: `fun describe(...): String { <blank> val label = when(...) {...} }` keeps its leading blank (first statement is a `val` declaration), while `fun findFirst(...): Int { <blank> items.forEach {...} }` and `return run search@ { <blank> for(...) {...} }` both strip it (first statement is not a declaration). New `KotlinSpecificRule.stripLeadingBlankBeforeNonDeclarationStatement`, wired into `Formatter.java` right after `stripOptionalSemicolons` in the Kotlin-only Phase 1 block: for every `{` that is not a named-construct body (already unconditionally blank-forced elsewhere), not a `when` body (own dedicated handling), and not a control-flow body (`if`/`while`/`for`/`catch`/`do`/`try`/`else`/`finally` -- STYLE.md's own "never add/remove blank lines inside a control-flow block" rule), if the first significant token after the brace is not `val`/`var`, any leading blank line in the gap is collapsed to a single newline. Verified this doesn't touch `kt_combined_inp.kt` (no leading-blank-after-`{` cases exist there at all) and doesn't fire on ordinary `if`/`while`/`forEach` bodies whose first statement merely *looks* declaration-adjacent (confirmed via ad hoc harness cases beyond the fixture itself). **Bug 4** (`BlockStructureRule.addClosingComments` and `insertNamedConstructBlankLines`, both shared classes): both methods gated their rewrite on `anyFrozen(tokens, openIdx, closeIdx + 1)` -- scanning the *entire* block span for any frozen token (a JXM_CFMT_DIS/ENA region) -- rather than just the small boundary window either method actually rewrites. `kt_comments_inp.kt`'s outermost `class Widget` contains `fun findFirstX`, which itself contains two JXM_CFMT_DIS/ENA regions nested deep inside; the whole-span scan found them and silently suppressed the *outer* class's own guaranteed blank line and closing comment, even though neither boundary gap of `class Widget`'s own `{`/`}` was ever going to be touched by either method. Fixed both by narrowing the frozen check to just the boundary gaps each method rewrites (`addClosingComments`: from the closing `}` through the comment-insertion point / existing-comment index; `insertNamedConstructBlankLines`: the open-side gap up to `beforeIdx` and the close-side gap from `afterIdx`). This is a genuine cross-language correctness fix, not Kotlin-specific -- confirmed it also silently affected the pre-existing, already-accepted `test/java_format_toggle_out.java` fixture (its outer `class FormatToggle` was likewise missing its blank line + `} // class FormatToggle` comment because of two unrelated frozen regions nested inside it), so updated that reference fixture too (added the blank line + closing comment) rather than leaving a second, undetected instance of the same bug uncovered by any regression test. `make test`: 34/34 before this session's changes (33 prior + `kt_combined`), 36/36 after (34 prior + `kt_combined` + `kt_comments`, the latter newly enabled in the `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES`), zero regressions across every existing C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture. Both the forward (`kt_comments_inp.kt` → `kt_comments_out.kt`) and idempotency (`kt_comments_out.kt` → `kt_comments_out.kt`) passes are green. |
| RDD_KEY_132 | Kotlin §8/§9 one-liner getter/setter grouping — `GetterSetterRule.groupOneLiners` never fired for Kotlin | Root cause (confirmed harness-broken in STATE_KOTLIN.md's Open Questions): `groupOneLiners`'s `isClassScope` gate was `lang.isJava || hasAccessSpecifier(...)`, never true for Kotlin (no C++-style `public:`/`private:` labels); more fundamentally `parseOneLinerMember`'s member-splitting (`splitMembers`, `;`/`{}`-terminator-based) and its modifier/name/return-type scanning both assume C/Java's `[modifiers] ReturnType name(...)` token order and `;`-terminated statements, neither of which hold for Kotlin's `[modifiers] fun name(...): ReturnType = expr` (name before type, newline-terminated, `;` optional). Fixed with the same "loosen shared-class visibility, then extend with a Kotlin-aware subclass" pattern as RDD_KEY_103/104: `GetterSetterRule`'s C/C++/Java-agnostic private helpers (`parseOneLinerMember`, `cellText`, `isInsignificant`, `firstSignificantIndex`, `nextSignificant`, `trimLeadingWs`, `trimTrailingWs`, `hasNewlineBetween`, `hasBlankLineRun`, `matchBracket`) raised to `protected` -- pure visibility changes, zero behavior change, confirmed via `make test` 36/36 before and after. New `KotlinGetterSetterRule extends GetterSetterRule`: its own `groupOneLiners` override (always-true class-scope posture, matching Java's own unconditional branch -- Kotlin has no scope distinction that would change this rule's applicability), a newline-terminated `splitKotlinMemberSpans` (int-index sibling of `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.splitKotlinStatements`), a Kotlin-shaped `parseKotlinOneLinerMember` recognizing `[modifiers] fun name(params) [: ReturnType] = expr` (returns null for anything else -- block-bodied functions, no-`=` signatures, multi-line members -- same "never guess past an unrecognized shape" posture as the rest of the Kotlin work), and its own `render` (three grid-aligned columns: `[modifiers] fun`, `name(params)` + `: ReturnType`, `= expr`, plus a ragged trailing-comment column) since the base class's `render` hard-codes a `{ body };` shape that doesn't exist in Kotlin's postfix-type, no-semicolon grammar. `Member.bodyFrom`/`bodyTo` (normally a brace-delimited block body) are repurposed to hold the expression-bodied function's `= expr` span, so `excludeOutliers`'s width-based outlier exclusion (STYLE.md §14's 3x-ratio rule) works unmodified and unmodified-inherited on the Kotlin path too -- confirmed via harness (a `"hello"`-bodied outlier among `1`/`22`-bodied siblings was correctly excluded from the group and left untouched, matching the same ratio rule Java/C++ already get). Scope: only expression-bodied one-liner functions (§9) -- the concrete shape harness-confirmed broken in Open Questions (`fun getX(): Int = 1`/`getY`/`getZ` failing to column-align); property accessor one-liners (`get()`/`set()`, §8) are a structurally different shape (no `fun` keyword, embedded inside a property declaration) and remain unhandled -- §8's "preserve as written" requirement is still satisfied (this class never touches anything outside its own `fun`-anchored shape), only the alignment upgrade for that specific shape remains a documented gap. New `test/kt_combined_inp.kt`/`kt_combined_out.kt` fixture case (`class Accessors { fun getX()... fun getLongName()... fun getZ()... }`, differing return types `Int`/`Int`/`String` to also exercise the return-type column with mixed widths) appended to the existing enabled fixture rather than creating a new fixture pair, per STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 4's own preference when an existing fixture already covers the same style area. `make test`: 36/36 before and after (72 PASS lines, forward + idempotency), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture. |
| RDD_KEY_133 | Kotlin §8 property-accessor (`get()`/`set()`) one-liner grouping — the remaining gap RDD_KEY_132 explicitly left open, harness-confirmed broken (`val x: Int` / `get() = 1` siblings never grouped, unlike §9's `fun`-based one-liners) | Scoped to the plain expression-bodied getter shape only: a `[modifiers] val/var name [: Type]` property declaration with **no initializer**, immediately (no blank line, no leading comment) followed by a bare `get() = expr` accessor line with **no** accompanying `set(...)`. Block-bodied accessors (`get() { ... }`/`set(v) { ... }`), any property with both an initializer and a custom accessor, and any getter+setter pair are all left completely untouched (still "preserved as written" per §8's own requirement — same scoping posture RDD_KEY_132 took for block-bodied functions). Design decision (no direct style-doc worked example for the *grouped* case, resolved by strong precedent from the task's own framing and from §9's already-established "collapse one-liner shapes into a grid" posture): the two-line source form is merged into a single Kotlin-legal line (`val x: Int get() = 1` is valid Kotlin) and the whole group column-aligned via `ColumnGrid`, mirroring §9's 4-cell shape (`[modifiers] val/var` / `name` / `: Type` / `get() = expr`) rather than trying to align only the second of two physical lines (which has no natural column to key off without a matching first-line cell). Implemented entirely inside `KotlinGetterSetterRule.java` (no new shared-class methods): `groupOneLiners` now tries `parseKotlinOneLinerMember` (§9) first per span, and on failure tries pairing the current span with the *next* one via new `parseKotlinAccessorMember` (§8); a run breaks not only on a blank line (as before) but also on a §8/§9 shape change between adjacent members (new `isAccessorMember`, keyed off `Member.pureSpecifier` repurposed as a shape marker "ACCESSOR", since that field is otherwise always null on the Kotlin path — avoids adding a new field to the shared `Member` class). `render` dispatches to a new `renderAccessorGroup` for §8 groups, reusing the same repurposed-`Member`-fields trick RDD_KEY_132 established (`bodyFrom`/`bodyTo` = the accessor's `= expr` span, `postParenQualifier` = the property's type text, `nameFrom`/`nameIdx` = the property name). Two real bugs found only via idempotency harness testing (formatting the merged output a second time) and fixed: (1) **shared-class fix**, `DeclarationAlignmentRule.needsSpaceBetween` — `get`/`set` are lexed `TokenType.KEYWORD` (Kotlin's soft-keyword list), not `IDENTIFIER`, so the existing "no space before `(` after an identifier" tight rule never fired for a merged line's own `get()`/`set()`, inserting a stray space (`get ()`) on every re-format; fixed with a Kotlin-gated (`lang.isKotlin`) carve-out treating `get`/`set` like an ordinary call name before `(`, pure no-op for C/C++/Java (neither keyword is reserved there). (2) `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.parseKotlinDeclaration`'s type-token scan (`while (... && !isOp(sig.get(i), "="))`) silently swallowed a re-parsed merged line's own `get()` tail into the type column (scanning up to the *first* `=` regardless of what precedes it), which on a second pass both corrupted that declaration's own type text and, more seriously, cross-contaminated an unrelated adjacent sibling declaration's column width when both were wrongly grouped together (found via harness: `var z: Int = 0` got its own type column padded out to match a wrongly-widened `Int get()` neighbor). Fixed by bailing (`return null`) the moment a bare `get`/`set` keyword is seen during the type scan — a `get`/`set` keyword can never legally appear inside a real type expression, so this is unambiguous positive evidence of a re-parsed §8 merge, not a guess; bailing leaves the already-correctly-rendered merged line as an untouched passthrough on subsequent passes, which is sufficient for idempotency (no active re-grouping needed on re-runs, since the text is already stable). Verified via a standalone harness (varying property-name widths mirroring §9's `getX`/`getLongName`/`getZ` precedent, an outlier-width case confirming `excludeOutliers`'s inherited 3x-ratio rule still works, a mixed §8+§9-adjacent-group case confirming the two shapes never merge into one group, block-bodied/setter/initializer cases confirming they stay untouched, and a trailing-same-line-comment case) — both the forward and idempotency (re-run-the-output) passes clean in every case. `test/kt_combined_inp.kt`/`kt_combined_out.kt` extended with a new `class PropertyAccessors { ... }` case (three plain getter one-liners with varying name widths, plus a `var count: Int = 0` / `set(v) { field = v }` block-bodied pair confirming it stays untouched and ungrouped) rather than a new fixture pair, same "extend the existing enabled fixture" precedent RDD_KEY_132 set. `make test`: 36/36 before and after (72 PASS lines, forward + idempotency), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture. |
| RDD_KEY_134 | Kotlin Step 5 dogfood testing (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`, via a persistent copy at `~/Projects/Shadow/rc_gui_frontend_android_DOGFOOD`) surfaced a compile-breaking bug, not just an idempotency mismatch: `RobotTcpSession.kt`'s `_startReaderThread` — a `Thread({ ...multi-statement, multi-line body... }, "tcp-reader")` call — had its entire trailing-lambda argument collapsed onto one physical line with no statement separators, producing text that would not compile (`... ?: break replyQueue.offer(line) ...` with no `;` or newline between `break` and `replyQueue`). Two sibling shapes in the same dogfood pass (`BleDeviceSelectDialog.kt`'s and `WifiStaDialog.kt`'s `postDelayed { ... }` scan-timeout callbacks) hit the identical bug | Root-caused (not guessed) via a minimal standalone repro reproducing `RobotTcpSession.kt`'s exact shape and `--diff`-ing it in isolation: `MiscRule.renderCallCandidate`'s Option 2 branch (`containsNewline(paramsSlice)` true, `splitTopLevelCommas(paramsSlice).size() > 1`) delegates to `renderCallPreserveGroups`, which in turn calls `groupByOriginalLine` — a helper that groups a multi-line call's *sibling arguments* by which original source line they started on, designed for C/C++/Java's ordinary "some args share a line, some don't" call-breaking shape. Its row-start detection (`pendingNewRow`, gated on `!currentHasSignificant`) only creates a new row when a newline is seen *before* any significant token has appeared in the current comma-delimited part — once a part's first significant token appears (immediately true for a lambda argument, whose very first token is `{`), every subsequent internal newline is invisible to the row-grouping logic. For a call like `Thread({ <10 lines of statements> }, "tcp-reader")`, this collapsed the entire multi-line lambda-body part plus the trailing `"tcp-reader"` argument into a *single* row, which `renderCallPreserveGroups` then rendered via `collapseTokensToOneLine` — safe for C/C++/Java (whose statements are always `;`-terminated regardless of physical line) but silently merging distinct Kotlin statements (which rely on the newline itself as the separator) with nothing between them. Existing single-argument-multiline bail (`splitTopLevelCommas(paramsSlice).size() <= 1 → return null`, documented inline as protecting a lone nested-call argument that itself wraps) did not cover this two-argument shape (a multi-line lambda *plus* a second, short sibling argument) since that check only fires when there is exactly one top-level argument total. Fixed by extending `renderCallCandidate`'s `containsNewline` branch with a **Kotlin-only** (`lang.isKotlin`) guard: after computing `splitTopLevelCommas(paramsSlice)`, bail (`return null`, leaving the whole call untouched byte-for-byte) if *any* individual top-level argument both contains a `NEWLINE` and contains a `{` — i.e., is itself a multi-line brace-bodied argument, regardless of how many total arguments there are. New `containsBrace` helper (sibling of the existing `containsNewline`) added for the check. Deliberately gated Kotlin-only rather than applied to all languages: an early attempt without the `lang.isKotlin` gate broke the pre-existing `real_code_regressions_1` C++ fixture (`combine(TE_PURE, some_variant(...), { ret, level1(ret) })`, a multi-line brace-init-list argument that legitimately depends on `renderCallPreserveGroups`'s reflow) — confirmed via `make test` regression, not assumed; C/C++/Java's own `;`-terminated statements make their multi-line brace-bodied arguments safe to reflow the existing way, so only Kotlin needed the more conservative bail. Verified: standalone repro (`Thread({ try { ... } catch(...) { ... } }, "tcp-reader")`) now stays untouched by this pass, confirmed idempotent (round1 output byte-identical to round2), and produces syntactically valid Kotlin (multi-statement lines still separated by their original newlines). Re-ran the dogfood pass end-to-end afterward: `RobotTcpSession.kt` and `WifiStaDialog.kt`'s `postDelayed` callback both dropped out of the round1-vs-round2 diff entirely (previously 9/46 dogfood files were non-idempotent; this fix alone resolved 2 of the 3 compile-breaking cases — `BleDeviceSelectDialog.kt`'s own `postDelayed` callback still shows an unrelated idempotency diff, not yet investigated). New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_17_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of `RobotTcpSession.kt`'s exact shape), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. `make test`: 36/36 before, 37/37 after (new fixture added), zero regressions across every existing C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture including `real_code_regressions_1` (confirmed the Kotlin-only gate keeps its C++ brace-init-list reflow intact). |
| RDD_KEY_135 | Follow-up dogfood idempotency bug (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`'s `PlayMusicBlock.kt`, found immediately after RDD_KEY_134 was fixed and re-run): `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.spansMultipleLines`'s blunt "any NEWLINE after the `=` token → bail out of the alignment group" check treated a call-wrapped-but-still-single-logical-statement initializer the same as a genuine multi-line block expression. `val display = if (full.length > MAX_LABEL_CHARS)\n    full.substring(...)... \nelse full` — on a fresh format, the braceless `if(cond)\n    stmt` shape is collapsed onto one line early (before decl-alignment runs), so decl-alignment sees a single line and groups it with the adjacent `val full`, padding both; `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` then wraps the now-too-long `full.substring(...)` call *after* the grouping decision was already made, producing correctly-padded, wrapped output. On a second format pass (the wrapped output as new input), decl-alignment now sees the pre-wrapped multi-line form directly and, since the wrapping newlines sit after the `=`, wrongly bails the whole declaration out of its group — silently shrinking `val full`'s own padding on every successive pass, a genuine idempotency bug (not observed as a compile-breaking one, but a visible style-flap RDD_KEY_134's own dogfood re-run surfaced) | Fixed `spansMultipleLines` with depth-aware newline classification, mirroring `ScopePipeline.hasTopLevelNewline`'s established "ignore newlines strictly inside a call's parens" idiom (added for the same reformat-of-already-formatted-output problem class): track paren/bracket depth and brace depth separately relative to the `=` token; a newline counts as "genuinely multi-line" (bail) only if it occurs at brace depth > 0 (inside a real `{`...`}` block/lambda body) or at both paren depth 0 and brace depth 0 (a true top-level newline with nothing explaining it away) — a newline sitting strictly inside a call's parens with no enclosing brace (parenDepth > 0, braceDepth == 0) is ignored, since that only means a brace-free initializer's own nested call got wrapped by a prior formatting pass, not that the initializer is a real block expression. **First attempt was too permissive and had to be corrected mid-session**: an initial version ignored *every* newline at depth > 0 regardless of brace nesting, which caused `make test` to regress the just-added RDD_KEY_134 fixture (`real_code_regressions_17`) — a `Thread({ ...multi-statement body... }, "tcp-reader")` trailing-lambda argument's own internal statement-separating newlines sit *inside* `Thread(`'s parens, so the over-permissive version silently swallowed them as "call-wrapping" too, letting decl-alignment's own flat-line rendering reintroduce the exact compile-breaking collapse RDD_KEY_134 had just fixed in `MiscRule`'s separate code path. Caught only via the regression test, not reasoned out in advance — confirms the value of RDD_KEY_134's fixture existing before this fix was attempted. The corrected brace-depth-aware version was then verified not to affect `kt_combined_out.kt`'s existing `val safe = x?.let { it + 1 } ?: 0` single-line-lambda case (no newline inside the braces at all, so neither carve-out changes its classification). New fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_18_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of `PlayMusicBlock.kt`'s exact shape, using a longer string literal to force the same call-wrapping RobotCoding's real code needed), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. Re-ran the dogfood round1-vs-round2 idempotency check afterward: `PlayMusicBlock.kt` and `BleDeviceSelectDialog.kt`'s `val filter` diff both dropped out entirely (2 more of the original 9 non-idempotent dogfood files resolved; 5 remain — `MainActivity.kt`, `BlockCanvasView.kt`, `BlockPalette.kt`, `MainViewModel.kt`, `BlePermissions.kt` — none yet root-caused; `BlockPalette.kt`'s remaining diff looks like an *analogous but distinct* bug in `KotlinGetterSetterRule`'s §9 one-liner function grouping, same column-width-flapping shape but a different class/code path, plus a separate Allman-brace idempotency gap in the same file, not fixed by this key). `make test`: 37/37 before, 37/37 after (new fixture 18 added, count unchanged since 17 was already counted), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture including `real_code_regressions_17`. |
| RDD_KEY_136 | Follow-up dogfood investigation (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`'s `MainActivity.kt`, per explicit "root-cause the closing-brace indentation drift next" direction after RDD_KEY_134/135 landed): a single-line round1-vs-round2 diff (closing `}` indent 16 vs 12 spaces) turned out to be a symptom of a deeper *first-pass* bug, not a pure idempotency mismatch — round1's own output for `_checkRecovery()` was already badly mis-indented against the known-correct original source. The shape: `AlertDialog.Builder(this).setTitle(...).setPositiveButton("Pulihkan") { _, _ -> if (blocks != null) { ... } else { ... } }` — a multi-line fluent call chain whose trailing lambda's `{` opens on a *continuation* line (`.setPositiveButton(...) {`), deeper than the chain statement's own first line (`AlertDialog.Builder(this)`). Minimal standalone repro (chained-call + trailing lambda + nested if/else, no `val`/AlertDialog specifics needed) reproduced the bug in isolation and confirmed it broken even on a fresh format, not just on reformat | Root-caused via a debug instrumentation pass (temporary `System.err` prints of each top-level span's `spanIndent`/`inheritedIndent` in `ScopePipeline.processScope`, removed before commit) showing the exact divergence: `ScopePipeline.findParentIndent` (used to derive `spanIndent`) deliberately anchors on the *whole statement's own first line* (walking back past `;`/`}` boundaries — needed elsewhere, e.g. for `case 1:` labels sharing a span with the statement after them), so for the trailing-lambda span it returned the chain's first line's indent (`AlertDialog.Builder(this)`, 8 spaces) rather than the lambda's own `{`-bearing continuation line's indent (`.setPositiveButton(...) {`, 12 spaces). `processScope` then used that under-shallow `spanIndent` both to compute the recursed-into child body's inherited indent (`spanIndent + indentUnit`, one level too shallow) and to force the span's own closing `}` back into alignment with it — under-indenting the lambda body by one level and consequently misplacing the nested if/else block's own closing braces to match. (The immediately-following `else`-body span additionally had `spanIndent == null` at this pipeline stage, since `Formatter.placeElseOnOwnLine` — which converts a still-K&R `} else {` to Allman — runs in a *later* phase than `ScopePipeline`; `findParentIndent`'s own inline comment already flagged this K&R-at-this-stage gap as returning "unknown" rather than crossing into the preceding statement's text.) Fixed by adding a new `ScopePipeline.braceLineIndent(tokens, idx)` helper — unlike `findParentIndent`, it derives indent purely from the *physical line containing `idx`* (scanning back to the nearest NEWLINE and taking its leading whitespace), with no statement-boundary reasoning, so it works identically whether the preceding token is a `{`-opening continuation line or (for the `else`-body span) a still-K&R `} else {`'s own physical line (which itself resolves back to the if-body's closing-brace line's indent — already correct, since that closing brace's line is real, un-reformatted source at this pipeline stage). In `processScope`, a new `effectiveSpanIndent` is computed as `braceLineIndent` when it is deeper than `spanIndent` (or when `spanIndent` is null and `braceLineIndent` is available), else falls back to `spanIndent` unchanged — used for the child body's inherited indent and the closing-brace force-reindent, while the *named-scope one-liner pre-expansion* logic (`struct Foo { a; b; };`) deliberately keeps using the original `spanIndent`, since that check is about the named construct's own indent and is unaffected by this bug class. Gated to Kotlin only (`lang.isKotlin`) via `braceIndent = lang.isKotlin ? braceLineIndent(...) : null`, out of caution: C/C++/Java's `;`-terminated, brace-anchored control-flow bodies don't commonly place a block-opening `{` at the end of a multi-line fluent chain the way a Kotlin trailing lambda does, and this fix's interaction with C/C++/Java's own multi-line-chain shapes (if any exist in practice) was not separately verified — `make test`'s full 38-fixture suite (all languages) passed unchanged before and after, confirming zero regressions for the gated languages regardless. New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_19_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of `_checkRecovery()`'s exact chained-builder-with-nested-if/else shape), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. Re-ran the dogfood round1-vs-round2 idempotency check afterward against the true original (unformatted) sources, not the stale pre-fix DOGFOOD copy (an early re-check against the DOGFOOD copy's already-round1-formatted, pre-fix content falsely looked unfixed — corrected by re-running against `~/Projects/RobotCoding/gui_frontend_android`'s pristine originals): `MainActivity.kt` and `BlePermissions.kt` are now idempotent, and their round1 output matches known-correct indentation against the original source (confirmed by inspection, not just round1-vs-round2 self-consistency). Two files remain non-idempotent for reasons unrelated to this key, both showing the same severe, apparently pre-existing statement-joining-without-separator bug (functions/statements concatenated onto one line with no newline or `;` between them, e.g. `"...text" _showMessage(` in `MainViewModel.kt` and `ToolbarActions.kt` alike) — not yet root-caused, likely a distinct bug in the same family as RDD_KEY_134 but a different code path/trigger shape; `ToolbarActions.kt`'s STATE_KOTLIN.md note of "incidentally resolved [by RDD_KEY_134/135], cause unconfirmed" turns out to have been about a *different*, now-fixed diff in that file (this key's closing-brace-drift class) — the statement-joining bug surfacing now was previously masked by/conflated with that other diff, not newly introduced by this fix. Both flagged in `STATE_KOTLIN.md` for future investigation, out of scope for this key. `make test`: 38/38 before, 38/38 after (fixture 19 added, prior count already included 17/18), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture. |
| RDD_KEY_137 | Follow-up dogfood investigation (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`'s `MainViewModel.kt`/`ToolbarActions.kt`, the "statement-joining-without-separator bug" RDD_KEY_136 flagged as surfacing once its own closing-brace-drift diff stopped masking it): a `val`/`var` declaration whose initializer is a parenthesized if/else expression (`val display = (if (cond) a else b)`), immediately followed by another statement in the same scope, was fused onto that following statement's line with no separator at all — compile-breaking on its face (`... else "") showMessage(` with no newline or `;` between them). Minimized via six progressively-simplified standalone repros (toggling parens presence, if/else presence, and string length independently) to the precise trigger: **both** the wrapping parens **and** the if/else inside them are required together — neither a parenthesized plain call nor an unparenthesized if/else alone reproduces it | Root-caused via targeted `System.err` instrumentation (temporary, removed before commit) dumping the reconstructed text after each of `Formatter.formatOne`'s top-level phase calls, bisecting to the exact phase where the separating newline vanished: `BlockStructureRule.collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`. That method's main dispatch loop fires on every `if`/`while`/`for` `KEYWORD` token unconditionally (`SINGLE_EXPR_KEYWORDS.contains(t.text)`), with no notion of Kotlin's `if`-as-value-expression (`val x = if (c) a else b`, unlike C/C++/Java where `if` is always a statement) — for the wrapped-if shape, `matchControlBlock` found the `if`'s own `(cond)` but no following `{` (a value-position `if`'s body is never braced the way a statement-position one can be), so it fell into the Kotlin-only "already-braceless, possibly multi-line body" branch (`tryCollapseBraceless`) meant for genuine braceless statement bodies (`if(x)\n    stmt`). That branch, and the separate bare-`else` dispatch branch a few lines below it (also gated only on `lang.isKotlin`, with no expression-position awareness of its own), both scanned forward past the if/else's own value arms and the *closing* wrapping paren, misreading the wrapping paren's close plus whatever followed as more of a "braceless body", consuming (and thus dropping) the real statement-separating newline after it. Fixed (Kotlin-only) by adding a running unmatched-`(`/`[`-depth counter to the main dispatch loop's own scan (incremented/decremented on every `(`/`[`/`)`/`]` token, mirroring `ScopePipeline.hasTopLevelNewline`'s established "track depth locally, ignore what's inside" idiom) and refusing to treat an `if` or bare `else` keyword as collapsible while that depth is > 0: a statement-position `if`/`else` is never itself nested inside a paren this same pass didn't open and fully consume via `matchControlBlock`'s own condition-matching (which always ends back at depth 0 relative to its own `if`), so depth > 0 at the keyword is unambiguous positive evidence of a *wrapped* value-position `if`, not a guess. **Deliberately does not** also gate on the if's immediately-preceding token (e.g. `=`) — an early, broader attempt doing exactly that regressed the pre-existing `real_code_regressions_18` fixture (`val display = if(full.length > MAX_LABEL_CHARS) full.substring(...) + "..." else full`, an *unparenthesized* expression-position `if` directly after `=`, which legitimately depends on this same braceless-collapse path per RDD_KEY_135's own established behavior and has no following-sibling-statement content within its own statement to over-consume) — confirmed via `make test` regression, not assumed; caught and reverted to the depth-only signal, which passes both. Verified: all six original repros (Repro6/7/10/11/12 plus the initial `ToolbarActions.kt`-shaped Repro6) now render correctly with the separating newline intact, confirmed idempotent (round1 output byte-identical to round2) for each. Re-ran the dogfood check afterward against the true pristine originals (`~/Projects/RobotCoding/gui_frontend_android`, not the stale DOGFOOD copy, per RDD_KEY_136's own established lesson): both `MainViewModel.kt` and `ToolbarActions.kt` are now idempotent, with `ToolbarActions.kt`'s `onCompileProgram`'s `val display = (if (...) ... else "") + "..."` line correctly rendered with `_showMessage(context, display)` on its own following line. New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_20_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of the minimal `(if (cond) a else b) + tail` shape immediately followed by a sibling call statement), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. `make test`: 39/39 before, 40/40 after (fixture 20 added), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture including `real_code_regressions_18` (confirmed the depth-only gate keeps its unparenthesized-if braceless-collapse path intact). |
| RDD_KEY_138 | Follow-up dogfood investigation (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`'s `BlockCanvasView.kt`, the "missing space before `&&`" diff STATE_KOTLIN.md flagged as a separate, still-unresolved item from RDD_KEY_134-137): a `val` declaration whose initializer contains a logical-AND expression lost the space before `&&`, rendering `sel.size > 1 && _pendingIndent >= 0` as `sel.size > 1&& _pendingIndent >= 0`. Not an idempotency-only bug — reproduced on a fresh first-pass format too, e.g. `val x = a > 1 && b` → `val x = a > 1&& b`. Minimized to the precise trigger: any `val`/`var` declaration (with or without an explicit type) whose initializer directly contains a top-level `&&`; plain assignment (`x = a && b`), `return a && b`, and function-call arguments (`foo(a && b)`) were all unaffected, isolating the bug to declaration-initializer rendering specifically | Root-caused by inspection (no instrumentation needed once the declaration-only scoping was established) of `DeclarationAlignmentRule.isTightToken`, the shared method `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderTokens`/`renderKotlinTokens` reuses (per RDD_KEY_103's earlier visibility-raise for exactly this reuse): its final line, `Token.isRepOp(t, '*') || Token.isRepOp(t, '&') || isOp(t, "::") || isOp(t, ".") || isOp(t, "->")`, was ungated by language. `Token.isRepOp(t, '&')` matches ANY `OP` token consisting solely of repeated `&` characters — written for C/C++'s repeated pointer/reference declarator sigils (`**` pointer-to-pointer, `&&` as an rvalue-reference type, e.g. `T&& x`), both of which are lexed as a single multi-char `OP` token by `TokenizerCore`. Kotlin's `&&` logical-AND operator is lexed identically (single `OP` token, text `"&&"`) but has an entirely different meaning — Kotlin has no unary/repeated `*`/`&` construct at all — so `isTightToken` wrongly reported `&&` as tight in Kotlin, suppressing the space `needsSpaceBetween` would otherwise have inserted before it. `MiscRule.isTightToken` (the equivalent shared-join-point method used by non-declaration expression rendering, e.g. `x * x` in a return/call context) already carried the identical `!lang.isKotlin &&` gate for this exact reason (its own inline comment cites `STYLE_KOTLIN.md §9`'s `= expr` rendering and a near-identical `x* x` bug) — `DeclarationAlignmentRule.isTightToken` was simply the one sibling copy that had never received the same gate, which is why the bug was scoped to declaration initializers specifically (the only rendering path that routes through `DeclarationAlignmentRule.renderTokens` instead of `MiscRule.isTightToken`) and not plain assignments/returns/call arguments (which route through `MiscRule`'s already-gated copy). Fixed by adding the identical `!lang.isKotlin &&` gate to `DeclarationAlignmentRule.isTightToken`'s `Token.isRepOp` checks, mirroring `MiscRule.isTightToken` verbatim. Verified: standalone repros (`val x = a > 1 && b`, `val x = a && b`, `val x: Boolean = a && b`) all now render with the space intact, and re-running the dogfood check against the pristine original `BlockCanvasView.kt` (not the stale DOGFOOD copy) confirmed it is now fully idempotent (round1-vs-round2 byte-identical) at both of the file's two previously-diffing locations. New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_21_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of the minimal `val ... = ... && ...` shape), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. Also separately noted, out of scope for this key: while authoring this fixture's explanatory comment, a multi-line `//` comment block containing several backtick-code-spans with a coincidentally-matching single-letter token at a line boundary triggered an unrelated pre-existing comment-alignment quirk (inserting large runs of spaces mid-sentence) — worked around by rewording the comment to avoid the coincidental trigger, not investigated further. `make test`: 40/40 before, 41/41 after (fixture 21 added), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture. |
| RDD_KEY_139 | Follow-up dogfood investigation (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`'s `BlockPalette.kt`, the "§9 one-liner-function column-width-flapping" diff STATE_KOTLIN.md flagged as a separate, still-unresolved item from RDD_KEY_134-138): a run of 10 adjacent §9 expression-bodied one-liner functions (`private fun _addMove() = ...`, etc.) had different column-alignment widths on round1 vs round2 — round1 padded all 10 to one uniform width, round2 split them into narrower subgroups. Reproduced with a 5-member minimal repro (three short bodies, one long-bodied call that gets line-wrapped, one more short body), confirming the flap is real and not specific to the full 10-member run | Root-caused by direct comparison against `GetterSetterRule.parseOneLinerMember`'s own doc comment, which already documents this *exact* bug class for the C/C++/Java base rule ("A body containing a non-empty-arg call ... check that predicted width here so grouping/padding is decided consistently whether this is a fresh format ... or a reformat of output already broken by that later phase ... without this check ... reformatting that already-broken output then correctly excludes the now-multi-line member via `hasNewlineBetween`, changing the surviving members' padding on the second pass"): on a fresh format, every member (including the eventually-too-long `_addPlayMusic()`) is still single-line at grouping time, so `KotlinGetterSetterRule.parseKotlinOneLinerMember`'s blunt `hasNewlineBetween` check passes for all 10 and the whole run groups/pads uniformly using each member's original (short) text; only afterward does `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` (a later pipeline phase) wrap the now-over-long padded line for `_addPlayMusic()`/`_addOperator()`/`_addWhile()` across multiple physical lines, leaving the group's padding decision stale relative to what's actually on disk. Reformatting that already-wrapped round1 output then correctly excludes those now-multi-line members via the same `hasNewlineBetween` check, splitting the previously-uniform run into narrower subgroups — a genuine idempotency flap, not a first-pass bug (round1's own output, while unstable under reformatting, is not itself wrong). `GetterSetterRule` had already solved this exact problem for its own (C/C++/Java) one-liner shape via a length pre-check in `parseOneLinerMember` — estimate the member's rendered width at its nesting depth and, if a call inside its body is "breakable" (non-empty-arg, so a later phase might wrap it) and the estimate exceeds `lineLengthLimit`, exclude it from the group on the very first pass too, keeping the decision (and thus the padding) stable across repeated formats — but this pre-check had simply never been ported to `KotlinGetterSetterRule.parseKotlinOneLinerMember`, its Kotlin sibling, which is why the bug was scoped to Kotlin's §9 shape specifically. **Shared-class fix**: raised `GetterSetterRule.indentWidth`/`lineLengthLimit` (fields) and `hasBreakableCall` (method) from `private` to `protected` (no behavior change, same "loosen shared-class visibility, then extend" pattern as RDD_KEY_103/104/133), then added the identical `hasBreakableCall(...)` + estimated-width pre-check to `parseKotlinOneLinerMember`, gated the same way as the base class (only applies when the body contains a breakable call at all, so short bodies with no call — or only zero-arg calls — are never wrongly excluded). Verified: the 5-member minimal repro and the full dogfood `BlockPalette.kt` file are both now idempotent (round1-vs-round2 byte-identical) at this diff's location; the surviving column widths match round1's own already-correct choice (the too-long member is excluded up front, same as it was on round2 previously, so the *smaller* remaining runs' width is what's now stable from the first pass on). New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_22_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of the minimal 5-member repro), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. `make test`: 41/41 before, 42/42 after (fixture 22 added), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture — confirms the C/C++/Java base rule's own `parseOneLinerMember` behavior (and its private `hasBreakableCall`/`indentWidth`/`lineLengthLimit` visibility raise) is unaffected. `BlockPalette.kt`'s second, unrelated diff (an Allman-brace idempotency gap for `override fun draw(...) { ... }` methods) remains unfixed, out of scope for this key. |
| RDD_KEY_140 | Follow-up dogfood investigation (RobotCoding `gui_frontend_android`'s `BlockPalette.kt`, the remaining "Allman-brace idempotency gap" diff RDD_KEY_139 explicitly flagged as out of scope): an `override fun draw(canvas: Canvas, x: Int, y: Int) { _drawBlock(canvas, x, y, fill, stroke, label) }` method body, nested inside an anonymous `object : Block() { ... }`, stayed K&R on round1 (with its call wrapped internally by a later phase) but flipped to Allman on round2. First repro attempt (built from a misremembered already-partially-wrapped shape, not the true original single-line source) was a false negative — already idempotent, not reproducing anything; corrected by rebuilding the repro from the real dogfood file's true original text | Root-caused by direct comparison against `JavaSpecificRule.isSingleLineBody`'s own doc comment, which already documents this exact bug class for the C/C++/Java base rule (same "predicted-width-before-later-wrapping-phase" shape as RDD_KEY_139, this time for `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceFunctionDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle`'s K&R-vs-Allman brace decision instead of one-liner column padding): on a fresh format the body is still genuinely one physical line at decision time, so `KotlinSpecificRule.isSingleLineBody` (which, unlike `JavaSpecificRule`'s already-fixed sibling, had never gained the width-prediction pre-check) kept `{` K&R; only afterward does `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` wrap the now-over-long call inside it, leaving the K&R choice stale. Reformatting that already-wrapped round1 output then correctly sees a genuinely multi-line body and moves `{` to Allman — an idempotency flap, not a first-pass bug. **Fix**: ported `JavaSpecificRule.isSingleLineBody`'s `hasBreakableCall` + estimated-width pre-check into `KotlinSpecificRule.isSingleLineBody`, this time as a **duplicated** local helper (not a shared/inherited one) since `KotlinSpecificRule` is a sibling of, not a subclass of, `JavaSpecificRule`/`CppSpecificRule` — adapted to this class's own existing helper API (`nextSignificantIndex(tokens, from)`, a single-argument, inclusive-of-`from` variant, called as `nextSignificantIndex(tokens, i + 1)` to actually advance past the starting token; `matchParenForward(tokens, openIdx)` in place of `GetterSetterRule.matchBracket`). A first port compiled cleanly but produced no observable effect: temporary `System.err` instrumentation (removed before commit) showed `hasBreakableCall` correctly firing but the estimated width still landing under `lineLengthLimit` for the true nested-object repro even though the real rendered line (111 characters) clearly exceeded it — traced to the estimated-width formula itself, copied verbatim from `JavaSpecificRule.isSingleLineBody`, which sums only `tokens.get(k).text.length()` across the body's tokens with **no allowance for indentation or inter-token spaces at all** — an undercount too small to matter at `JavaSpecificRule`'s shallower call sites (not separately re-verified there, out of scope for this key) but large enough at this Kotlin method's deeper, anonymous-object-nested indentation (12 spaces plus ~14 space-separated tokens, roughly 26 characters of pure whitespace omitted from the estimate) to produce a false "fits" verdict. Fixed by correcting the width formula to add `lineIndent(tokens, braceIdx).length()` up front and `+ 1` per non-whitespace/non-newline token (a one-space-per-token approximation, skipping actual WHITESPACE/NEWLINE tokens so they aren't double-counted), rather than porting `JavaSpecificRule`'s formula unmodified. Verified: both the minimal no-nesting repro (a plain `fun draw(...) { ... }` directly in a class body, deliberately padded past 100 characters) and the true nested-`object`-anonymous-method repro are now idempotent (round1 goes straight to Allman, round2 leaves it unchanged); re-ran the dogfood check against the pristine original `BlockPalette.kt` (not a stale copy, per RDD_KEY_136's established lesson) and confirmed the full file is now byte-identical round1-vs-round2. New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_23_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (a trimmed, self-contained version of the nested-`object`-anonymous-`override fun draw` repro), registered in `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` and documented in `test/README.txt`. `make test`: 42/42 before, 42/42 after (fixture 23 added, replacing the removed debug instrumentation before the final build), zero regressions across every C/C++/Java/Kotlin fixture — confirms the `JavaSpecificRule`/`GetterSetterRule` base-rule siblings' own (untouched) width formulas are unaffected, since this key's width-formula correction was scoped to `KotlinSpecificRule`'s local duplicate only. With this key resolved, both of `BlockPalette.kt`'s originally-flagged remaining diffs (RDD_KEY_139's column-width flap and this key's Allman-brace flap) are now fixed; per STATE_KOTLIN.md's Step 5, the next step is `./gradlew compileDebugKotlin` against the fully-formatted dogfood tree to confirm no compile regressions, not yet started. |
| RDD_KEY_141 | ./gradlew compileDebugKotlin against the fully-formatted RobotCoding gui_frontend_android dogfood tree (STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 5) failed with ~50 Kotlin compiler errors across 9 files, contradicting the previously-recorded clean baseline compile -- confirming the formatter itself introduces compile regressions | Root-caused via debug-instrumentation bisection (System.err.println after each Formatter.java pipeline step, then after each of ScopePipeline.processScope's five internal sub-passes) applied to a minimal repro (`x.foo().bar { it.f() }` inside a method body): the drop happens in ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass. That pass's Kotlin `: ReturnType` tail handling (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §9) calls findLastTopLevelCloseParen(tokens, span.start, span.openBraceIdx) to find the parameter list's own closing paren when it isn't the token immediately before the body's `{` -- but that helper only scanned for the LAST depth-0 `)` in the range, with no verification that a genuine top-level `:` actually appears between it and the brace. A fluent method chain of the shape `IDENTIFIER1().IDENTIFIER2 { lambda }` -- first call with genuinely empty parens, second call using Kotlin's bare/parenless trailing-lambda call syntax (valid Kotlin, no explicit `()`) -- has exactly the trigger token shape (a `)` followed eventually by an IDENTIFIER then `{`, no `:` anywhere), so the first call's `)` was wrongly accepted as a parameter-list close paren and the second call's name misread as a return-type tail, causing isCandidateSignatureName to pass and the whole span to be wrongly re-rendered as a function signature -- silently deleting the `.IDENTIFIER2` text from the output on the very first format pass (a first-pass correctness bug, not merely an idempotency flap, unlike every other Kotlin bug fixed earlier in this dogfood effort). Confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to RDD_KEY_140: reproduced identically against a standalone jar built from commit ad4f746 (immediately before RDD_KEY_140). Confirmed via direct comparison against real dogfood files: ConnectTypeDialog.kt and WifiApDialog.kt's `.show().also { ... }` calls were both collapsing to `.show() { ... }`, losing `.also`. **Fix**: in applySignaturePass's Kotlin branch, after findLastTopLevelCloseParen returns a candidate `)`, verify via nextSignificantIndex(tokens, realCloseParen) that the very next significant token is a top-level `:` -- if not, `continue` (bail, not a signature candidate) rather than accepting an unrelated preceding call's `)` as a return-type-tail marker. Verified against minimal repros in multiple shapes (bare lambda after empty-parens call now preserved; non-lambda second call, empty-parens second call, no-first-call, non-empty-first-call-args, and the Java equivalent were all already unaffected and remain unaffected) and against the two confirmed real dogfood files. New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_24_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`, registered in Makefile's INP_FILES and documented in test/README.txt. `make test`: 43/43 before, 43/43 after (fixture 24 added). Debug instrumentation added to Formatter.java and ScopePipeline.java during root-causing was fully reverted before this commit. Whether this single fix resolves all ~50 originally-seen compile errors across all 9 affected files, or whether some are separate bugs, not yet re-verified -- next step per STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 5 is to re-format the dogfood tree with the fixed jar and re-run ./gradlew compileDebugKotlin. |
| RDD_KEY_142 | Follow-up dogfood investigation (BlockCanvasView.kt's compile errors, following up on RDD_KEY_141): `class BlockCanvasView @JvmOverloads constructor(...)` was corrupted to `class BlockCanvasView@ JvmOverloads constructor(...)` -- a parse error, first-pass-broken not just an idempotency flap. Same root class also broke ToolbarActions.kt's *second* of two adjacent `@Volatile private var` declarations (`@ Volatile private var _lastSendLevel = ...`) even after RDD_KEY_141's fix. Root cause: `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceLabeledJumpSpacing`'s small state machine, built to detect Kotlin's `label@` loop-label declaration syntax (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §11, e.g. `outer@ for (...)`) and tighten the identifier/`@` gap, transitioned into its `AFTER_DECL_AT` state (which force-inserts a space after `@`) for ANY identifier immediately or newline-separated-ly followed by `@`, with no check that this was actually a label rather than an unrelated `@Annotation` -- a class name directly followed by a constructor annotation (`BlockCanvasView @JvmOverloads`), or an enum constant ending one statement immediately before an annotation starting the next (`= OptimizerLevel.NONE` then, on the next line, `@Volatile ...`). **Fix**: added a lookahead helper `isLoopLabelTarget(tokens, from)` requiring the token after the `@` to actually be `for`/`while`/`do` (a loop) or `{` (a labeled lambda literal, e.g. `"123".let parse@ { ... }`) -- the only constructs a Kotlin label can legally prefix -- before accepting an `IDENTIFIER @` sequence as a label declaration at all. Applied the guard to *both* the state-machine transition into `AFTER_DECL_AT` and to the separate `tightBeforeAt` computation that decides whether to actually tighten the gap before `@` -- fixing only the state transition first (an earlier attempt) left the tight-adjacency case (`class Foo @JvmOverloads constructor(...)`, gap is a single space with no newline) still broken, since that decision is made independently and doesn't consult the state-machine's own transition guard. Verified against three repros (adjacent `@Volatile` declarations across a newline; `class X @JvmOverloads constructor(`; and a genuine `outer@ for`/`break@outer`/`parse@ { ... }` labeled-lambda case, confirming all three remain correctly handled). New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_25_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`, registered in Makefile's INP_FILES and documented in test/README.txt. `make test`: 44/44 before, 44/44 after (fixture 25 added). Debug instrumentation added to Formatter.java and KotlinSpecificRule.enforceLabeledJumpSpacing during root-causing was fully reverted before this commit. Next step per STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 5: re-format the dogfood tree with this fix and re-run ./gradlew compileDebugKotlin to confirm BlockCanvasView.kt and ToolbarActions.kt now compile, then continue root-causing the remaining files (BlockPalette.kt's `startHolding`, MainToolbar.kt's `scrollX`/`scrollY`, XMLSaveLoad.kt's line-430 syntax error). |
| RDD_KEY_143 | Continued dogfood investigation (Optimizer.kt's compile errors, following up on RDD_KEY_142): a `when` expression's `else -> { ... }` arm with a multi-statement block body (`else -> { var redirected = n; for (...) { ... }; redirected }`) was flattened onto a single physical line with no `;` separators between the statements -- a parse error on the very first format pass, not an idempotency flap. Root cause: `BlockStructureRule.collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s bare-`else` handling, meant only for a genuine `if`/`else` chain's braceless single-statement body (STYLE.md §10, e.g. `if(x) return y; else return z;` collapsed from a braced multi-line form), matches ANY `KEYWORD "else"` not immediately followed by `if` or `{` -- with no check for whether this `else` is actually part of an `if`/`else` chain at all, versus a `when` expression's `else -> body` arm label, whose body is introduced by `->`, not an implicit brace-or-newline the way a real bare `else`'s single-statement body is. Once misidentified as a bare-`else` braceless body, `collapseBracelessBody` swallowed every statement inside the arm's `{ ... }` block onto one undifferentiated line with no separators inserted. **Fix**: added an `isWhenArrow` check (token immediately after `else` is `->`) alongside the existing `isElseIf`/`isBraced` checks, bailing out of the braceless-collapse path for that shape -- `KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions` already handles `else ->` arms correctly on its own and must run on untouched tokens. Verified against the flattened-block repro (now correctly left as a multi-line block) and two non-regressions: a value-bodied `else -> -1` when-arm (still collapses correctly, unaffected -- this shape was never broken since `next` there is `-1`, not `->`, so the new check doesn't change its behavior) and a genuine bare `if(...) {...} else {...}` statement (still braced, `isBraced` short-circuits before reaching the new check either way). New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_26_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`, registered in Makefile's INP_FILES and documented in test/README.txt. `make test`: 45/45 forward, 45/45 idempotency (fixture 26 added, fixture 25 from RDD_KEY_142 still passing). Per STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 5, dogfood-tree gradlew compileDebugKotlin run before this fix (post-RDD_KEY_142) showed exactly 3 files still failing: Optimizer.kt (this bug, line 564), ProgramBuilder.kt (lines 122-124, 239 -- **correction**: independent investigation found these are NOT the same `else -> { multi-statement }` shape; see RDD_KEY_144 for ProgramBuilder.kt's actual, unrelated root causes), and XMLSaveLoad.kt (line 430, a still-unconfirmed distinct shape). Next step: rebuild jar, re-format dogfood tree, re-run gradlew compileDebugKotlin to confirm the whole tree compiles, then root-cause XMLSaveLoad.kt's line-430 error if it remains. |
| RDD_KEY_144 | Continued dogfood investigation (ProgramBuilder.kt's compile errors, following up on RDD_KEY_143): TWO separate, unrelated bugs co-occurring in the same statement (`_items.filter { it !is _FunctionItem || calledFunctions.contains(it.func.funcName) }`), caught by carefully comparing the isolated repro's output at each corruption point rather than assuming a shared root cause. **Bug A**: Kotlin's `!is`/`!in` negated type-check/containment operators are a single tight lexical unit with no space between `!` and the keyword (unlike plain `a is B`, always spaced), but the tokenizer still lexes them as two separate tokens, and `DeclarationAlignmentRule.needsSpaceBetween` had no case for this pair -- the generic KEYWORD-gets-a-leading-space default fired, corrupting `!is`/`!in` into `! is`/`! in`, a parse error. **Fix**: added a Kotlin-gated check in `needsSpaceBetween` (`lang.isKotlin && isOp(prev, "!") && cur.type == KEYWORD && ("is"/"in").equals(cur.text)`) returning `false`. **Bug B**: `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s `renderCallCandidate` uses `parseSignature` (a C/C++/Java-style "type name" declaration parser) to distinguish a real forward-declaration's parameter list from a plain call's argument list -- both look like `IDENTIFIER ( ... )` at this scan's level. Kotlin has no such prototype-only declaration shape at all (Kotlin function signatures always start with `fun`, use reversed `name: Type` order, and are handled entirely by `ScopePipeline`, not this pass). Left unguarded, `parseParam`'s generic "last IDENTIFIER is the name, everything before it is the type" heuristic misparsed the ordinary call argument `it.func.funcName` (no top-level comma) as if it were a `Type name` pair (type `it.func.`, name `funcName`), causing the typed `renderDropped`/`renderOnePerLine` render path to insert a column-separator space between them once the line needed wrapping -- corrupting `it.func.funcName` into `it.func. funcName`. **Fix**: split into two variables -- `sig` (computed normally via `parseSignature`, still drives the existing zero-param bail-out, since a Kotlin function's own empty `()` param list must never be wrapped either) and a new `sigForRender` (forced `null` only for Kotlin, used only to select which render path -- typed declaration-style vs. untyped call-style -- the remaining wrap logic uses). **Regression caught and fixed during Bug B's first attempt**: initially gated `parseSignature` off entirely for Kotlin (disabling the zero-param bail-out too), which broke fixture 22 (`real_code_regressions_22_inp.kt`, RDD_KEY_140's `startHolding` fixture) by wrongly wrapping a real Kotlin function's own empty `()` into `(\n    \n)`; caught immediately by `make test` and fixed via the `sig`/`sigForRender` split described above. Verified via isolated repro (`it !is _FunctionItem || calledFunctions.contains(it.func.funcName)`): both corruptions fixed simultaneously, output shows `!is` intact and `it.func.funcName` wrapped with no inserted space. New permanent fixture pair `test/real_code_regressions_27_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`, registered in Makefile's INP_FILES and documented in test/README.txt. `make test`: 47/47 forward, 47/47 idempotency (fixture 27 added, all prior fixtures including 22 and 26 still passing -- confirms no regressions). Both fixes are gated to `lang.isKotlin` (Bug A) or apply only to the Kotlin-forced `sigForRender` path (Bug B), so no C/C++/Java behavior changed. |
| RDD_KEY_145 | Real-code testing against `square/okio` (STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 5's next queued candidate, a pure Kotlin/JVM library needing no Gradle-copy dance): first pass found a large (~43 file) round1-vs-round2 idempotency diff, most of which turned out to be a test-methodology mistake, not a formatter bug -- testing at the formatter's default `indent-size` (4) against a project whose own `.editorconfig` specifies `indent_size=2` produced spurious-looking indentation corruption in every wrapped multi-line signature. Re-running with a `.jxmake-code-formatter` override (`indent-size=2`) matching okio's own convention eliminated the vast majority of the diff (down to 7 files), per STATE_COMMON.md's methodology guidance to match a candidate's own convention before concluding "no bug". No code change; recorded so the same false-positive pattern isn't rediscovered against a future candidate. |
| RDD_KEY_146 | Real bug found via `square/okio` real-code testing (see RDD_KEY_145): `val prefixIndex = -1`-shaped Kotlin declaration initializers rendered as `= - 1`, an extra inserted space between a leading unary minus and its operand. Root cause: `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s declaration-initializer re-render path (`renderKotlinTokens`, reused from `renderTokens`) delegates spacing to the base `DeclarationAlignmentRule.needsSpaceBetween`, a strictly pairwise `(prev, cur)` check with no visibility into the token before `prev` -- it cannot distinguish a leading/prefix unary `-`/`+` from a binary one, and unconditionally spaces `-` from a following `IDENTIFIER`/`NUMBER`. **Fix**: raised `DeclarationAlignmentRule.needsSpaceBetween`'s visibility from `private` to `protected` (purely additive, no behavior change, same pattern as RDD_KEY_103); added `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderTokens` as a new `@Override` (the base class had none to override before) that wraps `needsSpaceBetween` with a new `isUnaryMinusOperand(tokens, index)` lookback -- true iff `tokens[index-1]` is `-`/`+` and is not itself preceded by an operand (`IDENTIFIER`/`NUMBER`/closing `)`/`]`), which would make it binary instead. Scoped entirely to `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s own override, so C/C++/Java's separate `renderInitTokens` path is untouched. `make test`: 49/49 before, 49/49 after. |
| RDD_KEY_147 | Second real bug found via `square/okio` real-code testing (see RDD_KEY_145): `ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass`'s Kotlin `: ReturnType` tail detection wrongly merged a headerless multiplatform declaration (`expect fun getEnv(name: String): String?`, no body of its own) with a later, unrelated declaration (`val okioRoot: Path by lazy { ... }`) separated from it by a blank line -- the top-level-`)`-then-`:` scan had no check that the span between the matched `)` and the eventually-found `{` stayed within one statement, so it silently walked across the blank line and ate the `expect fun`'s own text plus the `val`'s leading tokens into one bogus signature+tail. **Fix**: added a new `hasTopLevelBlankLine(tokens, from, to)` helper (true iff two consecutive `NEWLINE` tokens appear in the range, ignoring `WHITESPACE`) and a guard immediately after the existing `:` check in `applySignaturePass` that bails (`continue`) rather than accepting the tail if a blank line is found between the candidate `)` and the span's open brace -- a genuine `: ReturnType` tail is a single unbroken type expression and never itself contains a blank line. Shared-class change (`ScopePipeline.java` is on the Hard Constraint list) but not itself Kotlin-gated, since the entire surrounding branch is already Kotlin-only (this `:` tail detection has no C/C++/Java equivalent). `make test`: 49/49 before, 49/49 after. |
| RDD_KEY_148 | Third real bug found via `square/okio` real-code testing (see RDD_KEY_145), the last of the original 7-file diff: a braceless `if (dataSize < requiredSize) { throw IOException("...") }` (`ZipFiles.kt`) round1-vs-round2 flapped between one line and a multi-line wrap. Root cause: both `BlockStructureRule.tryCollapse` (brace-removing collapse of an existing `{ statement; }` block) and `tryCollapseBraceless` (collapse of an already-braceless body) render their `keyword (condition)` prefix via `renderInline`, which preserves the *original* source's keyword-to-`(` space (`"if ("`) verbatim -- one character wider than the tightened `"if("` form a later `MiscRule`/`TIGHT_PAREN_KEYWORDS` pass produces. `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s length check (`wholeLine.length() <= lineLengthLimit`) runs on this stale, wider text, so a line whose true final tightened width sits exactly at the 100-char limit gets wrongly over-wrapped on the first pass; reformatting that (wrongly) already-wrapped output then correctly measures the by-then-tightened line and collapses it back -- an idempotency bug, same "decision made before a later pass runs" class as RDD_KEY_128/139/140. **Fix**: new `BlockStructureRule.TIGHT_PAREN_KEYWORDS` (own private copy of `MiscRule.TIGHT_PAREN_KEYWORDS`'s keyword set -- `if`/`while`/`for`/`switch`/`catch`/`when` -- kept separate since the two classes have no other coupling) and a new `tightenParenPrefix(keyword, rendered)` helper that strips the single space between `keyword` and `(` if present; called from both `tryCollapse` and `tryCollapseBraceless` right after their existing `renderInline` call. Not Kotlin-gated -- `SINGLE_EXPR_KEYWORDS`/`TIGHT_PAREN_KEYWORDS` and both collapse methods are unconditional across all four languages, and the bug is a genuine cross-language correctness issue (confirmed via `make test`: 49/49 before, 49/49 after, no regressions). New combined permanent fixture `test/real_code_regressions_30_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` covering all three of RDD_KEY_146/147/148 in one file (verified individually reproducing/fixing all three, plus round1-vs-round2 idempotent), registered in Makefile's INP_FILES and documented in test/README.txt. `make test` after fixture add: 50/50 forward, 50/50 idempotency. A fourth, distinct bug was also found in the same okio pass (see RDD_KEY_149) but left unfixed/deferred -- see STATE_KOTLIN.md's Open Questions. |
| RDD_KEY_149 | Fourth bug found via `square/okio` real-code testing (see RDD_KEY_145), found but **not fixed** -- deferred per STATE_COMMON.md's ambiguity protocol pending deeper investigation than this pass's time budget allowed. Symptom: a multi-line Kotlin function parameter list whose params are individually rendered via `KotlinSignatureRule.render`'s `ColumnGrid`-based path (confirmed via debug instrumentation to correctly build column-padded lines, e.g. `"string     : String,"`/`"beginIndex : Int,"`/`"endIndex   : Int,"`) ends up in the *final written file* with the column padding collapsed to a single space (`"string : String,"`) and the trailing comma stripped -- neither of which `render()`/`renderWithTail()` themselves produce (confirmed by a temporary `System.err` debug print directly at the point `render()` builds each line, immediately before returning). This means something *downstream* of the signature renderer's return value -- after its `Replacement` is spliced back into the overall token/text stream -- re-normalizes or re-tokenizes this already-rendered text and silently drops both the alignment padding and the trailing comma; grepped the codebase for an explicit trailing-comma-stripping pass and found none, so the mechanism is still unidentified (candidate theory: some later whole-file pass re-tokenizes literal `WHITESPACE` runs inside already-rendered replacement text down to a single space, not realizing multi-space runs there are intentional column padding, though this doesn't yet explain the comma loss). Reproduces with a minimal 3-param repro (`RealBufferedSink.kt`'s `commonWriteUtf8(string: String, beginIndex: Int, endIndex: Int)`) and confirmed to hit the *exact same* rendered form as if the whole signature had gone through `render()`'s "fits inline, collapse to one line" path instead -- i.e. round1's multi-line-but-unpadded-and-comma-stripped form is a hybrid of both code paths' output, not a clean product of either. Also reproduces in `FakeFileSystem.kt`. Debug instrumentation (`ColumnGrid.flush`'s `CG_DEBUG` env-gated print, `KotlinSignatureRule.render`'s per-line `KSR_DEBUG` print) was added during investigation and fully reverted before committing -- none remains in the source. Left as an Open Question in STATE_KOTLIN.md rather than guessed at further. |
| RDD_KEY_150 | Real compile-breaking bug found via `kotlinc` compile-checking against `square/okio` (not caught by round1-vs-round2 idempotency diffing, since broken consistently from the very first format pass): `TokenizerCore.MULTI_CHAR_OPS` had no entry at all for Kotlin's `===`/`!==` referential equality/inequality operators, only their 2-char value-equality prefixes `==`/`!=`. `if (next !== this)` (`Segment.kt`) lexed as the two separate tokens `!=` and `=`, which a later paren-tightening pass then re-spaced into the invalid `!= =`, a parse error. **Fix**: added `"===", "!=="` to `MULTI_CHAR_OPS`, positioned ahead of `"==", "!="` per the array's existing longest-prefix-first requirement. Shared class (`TokenizerCore.java` is on the Hard Constraint list) but not gated -- `===`/`!==` don't occur in valid C/C++/Java source, so this is additive with no realistic collision. `make test`: 50/50 before, 50/50 after. Combined into `test/real_code_regressions_31_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` with RDD_KEY_151. |
| RDD_KEY_151 | Second compile-breaking bug found via the same `kotlinc` compile-check pass (see RDD_KEY_150): `BlockStructureRule`'s braceless single-statement `if`/`while`/`for` collapse dispatch treats every `KEYWORD "while"` as a candidate loop-start (condition `(...)` plus optional body), with no check that this `while` is actually the trailing keyword of a preceding `do { ... } while (cond)` -- a do-while's own trailing `while (cond)` has nothing after its `)` but a statement terminator, the exact same shape (`block.openBraceIndex < 0`) as Kotlin's already-braceless-but-multi-line body (RDD_KEY_127's shape). `Buffer.kt`'s `} while (!done && head != null)` followed (after a blank line) by an unrelated `size -= seen.toLong()` got misread as that `while`'s own braceless body and fused onto the same physical line with no separator. **Fix**: new `isDoWhileTailKeyword(tokens, whileIdx)` lookback -- true iff the nearest preceding significant token is a `}` whose matching `{` is itself immediately preceded by a `do` keyword -- gating the `SINGLE_EXPR_KEYWORDS` dispatch entry for `while`. Not Kotlin-gated (the guard only ever changes behavior for a `while` immediately following a `}`-closed `do` block with no intervening `{`, which for C/C++/Java always has a `;` right after the `)` and so never reaches the Kotlin-only braceless branch in the first place -- confirmed inert for those languages via `make test`: 50/50 before, 50/50 after). Combined into `test/real_code_regressions_31_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` with RDD_KEY_150; `make test` after fixture add: 51/51 forward, 51/51 idempotency. |
| RDD_KEY_152 | Real-code testing against `square/kotlinpoet` (Step 5's next queued candidate, `.editorconfig` `indent_size=2`, no Gradle-copy dance needed -- pure Kotlin/JVM library): round1-vs-round2 idempotency diffing found 13 non-idempotent files out of 131; root-caused and fixed one shared root cause covering (at least) one of them. A `when` branch whose own body is a nested `when { ... }` (e.g. `TypeName.kt`'s `get(type: Type, ...)`: `is Class<*> ->` followed by `when {` on the next line in the original source) had its closing `}`'s indentation flap between two different columns across rounds. Root cause: `ScopePipeline`'s Kotlin-only `braceLineIndent` (RDD_KEY_136) anchors a scope's indent on the physical line its opening `{` sits on at the moment `ScopePipeline` runs, early in the pipeline (Phase 0) -- but `KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions`' arrow-alignment pass, much later (Phase 4), merges the branch's label line and the nested `when {` onto one physical line whenever the merged line still fits the length limit, which it always does here since the body's own first line is just the bare `{`. A fresh format computes the indent against the PRE-merge physical line; a reformat of the already-merged output computes it against the POST-merge physical line -- disagreeing by one indent level. **Fix**: new `ScopePipeline.findMergingWhenBranchLineStart` lookahead, called from `braceLineIndent`, Kotlin-gated -- detects the exact shape (a lone `when {` line whose only content is the `when` keyword plus the brace, immediately preceded by a physical line ending in a top-level `->`) and anchors on the eventual post-merge line up front, so both a fresh format and a reformat of merged output agree. `make test`: 51/51 before, 52/52 after (new fixture `test/real_code_regressions_32_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`). Two more root causes remain in the same 13-file diff (a call-argument continuation-line indent staleness in `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`, and a `KotlinSignatureRule` parameter-list-flattening flap), both showing the same "Phase 4 arrow-merge invalidates an earlier phase's physical-line-anchored decision" shape but in different rule classes -- not yet root-caused to the same depth as this one; left open, see STATE_KOTLIN.md. |
| RDD_KEY_153 | Second bug found via `square/kotlinpoet` real-code testing (see RDD_KEY_152), this one a genuine first-pass, compile-breaking bug caught by `kotlinc` syntax-checking `kotlinpoet/src/jvmMain` (not by round1-vs-round2 idempotency diffing -- broken consistently from the very first format pass, same category as RDD_KEY_150/151's okio-session discovery method). `TypeSpecHolder.kt`'s `public fun addTypes(typeSpecs: Iterable<TypeSpec>): T = apply { for (typeSpec in typeSpecs) addType(typeSpec) } as T` (an expression-bodied function whose `= expr` is itself a trailing-lambda call to `apply`) had `apply`'s own `{` wrongly split onto its own line as if it were the enclosing function's own body brace: `= apply` on one line, `{ for(typeSpec in typeSpecs) addType(typeSpec)} as T` on the next -- not valid Kotlin (a bare `apply` followed by a newline then `{ ... }` is not a trailing-lambda call, it's two separate, invalid top-level constructs). Root cause: `KotlinSpecificRule.findSignatureCloseParenBeforeBrace`'s backward scan (meant to walk back over a `: ReturnType` clause between a candidate body `{` and its signature's `)`) had no bail-out when it hit a depth-0 `=` before finding the `:` it was looking for -- so scanning backward from `apply`'s `{` past `apply` itself, then `=`, then `T`, it eventually reached the function's own real `: T` and `)`, incorrectly concluding `apply`'s `{` WAS this function's own body brace (`enforceFunctionDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle`'s Allman-conversion candidate signal is otherwise deliberately conservative, per RDD_KEY_114, specifically to avoid exactly this class of misfire -- this was a gap in that conservatism). **Fix**: added a `depth == 0 && isOp(t, "=")` bail-out (return -1) to `findSignatureCloseParenBeforeBrace`'s scan loop, ahead of the existing `:` check -- a depth-0 `=` before any `:` unambiguously means this is an expression-bodied function's `= expr` tail, not a `: ReturnType` span, so the `{` under investigation can never be this function's own body. Kotlin-only file (`KotlinSpecificRule.java` is not on the Hard Constraint shared-class list), no cross-language regression risk. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_33_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 52/52 before, 53/53 after. |
| RDD_KEY_154 | Real-code testing against `Kotlin/kotlinx.coroutines` (Step 5's next queued candidate, `kotlinx-coroutines-core`'s `common`+`jvm` source sets, 163 files, `.editorconfig` `indent_size=4` matching this tool's default -- no config override needed): round1-vs-round2 idempotency diffing found 11 non-idempotent files. Root-caused and fixed 1: an expression-bodied function whose `=` is immediately followed by a `{`-led lambda-literal body (e.g. `Deprecated.kt`'s `internal fun consumesAll(...): CompletionHandler = \n { cause -> ... }` and `internal fun ReceiveChannel<*>.consumes(): CompletionHandler = { cause -> ... }`) has that `{...}` body left unconsumed as `FunctionTail.exprTokens` (empty), by design, since the tail parser can't distinguish a lambda-literal expression body from an ordinary block body at that point. `KotlinSignatureRule.renderWithTail`'s `exprStr` computation unconditionally rendered `"= " + renderTokens(exprTokens)`, baking a trailing space onto the `=` even when `exprTokens` was empty -- and since the untouched remainder (the lambda body's own original leading whitespace before its `{`/next line) is preserved as-is downstream, re-splicing this baked trailing space on top of it added one more space of gap on every re-format pass (non-idempotent, unboundedly growing whitespace). **Fix**: `exprStr` now renders as bare `"="` (no trailing space) when `exprTokens` is empty, letting the untouched remainder's own original single space/newline serve as the separator instead of stacking a second one. Not Kotlin-gated by construction (`KotlinSignatureRule.java` is Kotlin-only, not on the Hard Constraint shared-class list).
