New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_37_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 56/56 before, 57/57 after. 10 diffs remain across the 163-file candidate (`BufferedChannel.kt`, `AbstractSharedFlow.kt`, `ChannelFlow.kt`, `ConcurrentLinkedList.kt`, `ThreadSafeHeap.kt`, `JobSupport.kt`, `Select.kt`, `DebugProbesImpl.kt`, `ExceptionsConstructor.kt`, `SystemProps.kt`) -- surveyed but not root-caused this session; at least two distinct shapes observed (a closing-`}`-indentation drift, and a `try`/`catch`-as-expression K&R/Allman brace-style + indentation confusion in `SystemProps.kt`'s `systemProp()`), left open per STATE_COMMON.md's ambiguity protocol rather than guessed at further -- see STATE_KOTLIN.md Open Questions. `kotlinc`/full Gradle compile-check not run this session; only the lightweight PSI-based `kotlin_sc` syntax-only tool was used per user request (see caveat in STATE_KOTLIN.md's Step 5 section: it does not do semantic/type checking and cannot catch a formatter bug that produces syntactically-valid-but-semantically-wrong Kotlin). |
| RDD_KEY_155 | `Kotlin/kotlinx.coroutines` compile-check bug #1 (found via the lightweight PSI-based `kotlin_sc` syntax-only tool, not idempotency diffing -- broken consistently from the first pass): `Guidance.kt`'s KDoc contains its own code example with a literal `/* what to do if this coroutine fails */` snippet nested inside the outer `/** ... */` doc-comment -- valid Kotlin, since (unlike C/C++/Java) Kotlin block comments nest. `TokenizerCore.emitBlockComment` had no notion of nesting -- it closed the outer doc-comment at that first inner `*/`, after which everything else in the file was mis-lexed as ordinary code, corrupting/truncating the output (~330 lines silently dropped from a 505-line file). **Fix**: Kotlin-gated nesting-depth tracking added to `emitBlockComment` (increments on `/*`, decrements on `*/`, stops at depth 0); C/C++/Java path unchanged (still closes at the first `*/`, matching those languages' real grammar, where block comments don't nest). Shared-class change (`TokenizerCore.java`), Kotlin-gated. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_38_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 57/57 before, 58/58 after. |
| RDD_KEY_156 | `Kotlin/kotlinx.coroutines` compile-check bug #2 (same `kotlin_sc` pass as RDD_KEY_155): a qualified-this label reference, `this@Label` (e.g. `BroadcastChannel.kt`'s `select.trySelectDetailed(this@BroadcastChannelImpl, Unit)`), had a space wrongly inserted between `this` and `@` by some earlier generic keyword-spacing pass, producing `this @BroadcastChannelImpl` -- a real syntax error (`Label must be named`/`Expecting an element`). Root cause: `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceLabeledJumpSpacing`'s §11 state machine (RDD_KEY_105/142) only recognized a jump keyword (`return`/`break`/`continue`) or a plain identifier as eligible to sit tight before `@`; the `this` keyword fell through to `state = JumpState.NONE`, so this pass never removed the earlier-inserted gap. **Fix**: new `AFTER_THIS_KEYWORD`/`AFTER_THIS_AT` states -- `this` immediately followed by `@` (unambiguous, unlike a plain identifier's `label@`, since `this` is never followed by an unrelated `@Annotation`) is tightened through the label identifier, with no forced trailing space afterward (unlike a jump's `@label`, which is always followed by a value expression). Kotlin-only file (`KotlinSpecificRule.java`), no cross-language risk. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_39_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 58/58 before, 59/59 after. Combined with RDD_KEY_155's fix, the `Kotlin/kotlinx.coroutines` `common`+`jvm` (163 files) `kotlin_sc` pass drops from 9 files with syntax errors to 1 (`LimitedDispatcher.kt`, not yet root-caused -- see STATE_KOTLIN.md Open Questions). |
| RDD_KEY_157 | Follow-up (`LimitedDispatcher.kt`'s last remaining `kotlin_sc` error, after RDD_KEY_155/156): `obtainTaskOrDeallocateWorker()`'s `while (true) { when (val nextTask = ...) { null -> synchronized(workerAllocationLock) { runningWorkers.decrementAndGet(); if (queue.size == 0) return null; runningWorkers.incrementAndGet() } ... } }` had the entire three-statement `synchronized(...) { ... }` body fused onto one physical line with no separators (Kotlin has no mandatory `;`) -- a first-pass syntax error, reproduces on a fresh format, not merely an idempotency flap. Minimal repro isolated directly from this file (a synthetic `class Foo { ... }` wrapper around the same construct), formatted standalone against a locally built jar with temporary debug prints bracketing each `Formatter.formatOne` phase (removed before commit) -- confirmed the fusion is already present immediately after `BlockStructureRule.collapseSingleExpressionBlocks` (the very first phase-1 pass), before any later `KotlinSpecificRule` pass runs; ruled out `formatWhenExpressions`/`applyArrowAlignment` (only rewrite the branch's own label+arrow span, never `bodyStart..bodyEnd`) and `MiscRule.renderCallCandidate`'s Option 2 multi-line-argument path (the trailing lambda sits outside `synchronized`'s own parens, never reached), matching the prior session's own ruled-out list. Root cause: the `while`'s sole body statement is syntactically one statement (the `when` expression) per `BlockStructureRule.isKotlinSingleStatementBody`'s Kotlin-only newline-boundary check -- correctly so -- but that check has no notion that the single statement can itself own a nested `{...}` block containing several further statements of its own (here, the `when` branch's `synchronized(...) { ... }` body); `tryCollapse`'s `renderInline` flattens every whitespace/newline in the collapsed span to a single space with no brace-depth awareness at all, so approving the outer collapse also silently flattens that unrelated nested block's separate statements onto the same line. **Fix**: new `BlockStructureRule.containsMultilineNestedBrace` helper (tracks `{`/`}` depth, true if any NEWLINE token appears at depth > 0) called at the top of `isKotlinSingleStatementBody` -- bails out of the single-statement/collapsible classification whenever the body contains such a block, regardless of how many statements it holds. Shared-class file (`BlockStructureRule.java`), but the new check only gates the Kotlin-only branch of `isSingleStatementBody` (the `lang.isKotlin` arm calling `isKotlinSingleStatementBody`) -- inert for C/C++/Java, which never reach that arm. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_40_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 60/60 before and after (fixture included in the "after" count). Re-ran `kotlin_sc` narrowly against just the real `LimitedDispatcher.kt` (round1 of the full file, not the whole 163-file tree) post-fix: confirmed clean (`OK: no syntax errors`). | RDD_KEY_161 | Continued `Kotlin/kotlinx.coroutines` idempotency investigation (2 files left open by RDD_KEY_160): root-caused and fixed `BufferedChannel.kt`'s bare `}` closing-brace drift inside `onUndeliveredElementReceiveCancellationConstructor`'s unnamed nested-lambda-chain (`onUndeliveredElement?.let { { select, _, element -> { _, _, _ -> ... } } }`), confirmed by inspection in RDD_KEY_159/160 NOT to share the named-scope root cause fixed there. Root-caused via debug prints in `ScopePipeline.findParentIndent`/`processScope` (temporary `SP_DEBUG`/`FPI_DEBUG`-gated, removed before commit): `findParentIndent`'s `spanIndent` for the `let {`'s own span read `null` on round1 (correctly falling back to `braceIndent`, col 4) but a bogus 8-space value on round2 (wrongly winning over `braceIndent` under the "deeper wins" unnamed-scope heuristic). Traced further to `splitTopLevelSpans`: a braceless `if (...) { ... } else expr` statement (as in the preceding, unrelated `processResultSelectReceiveOrNull` sibling declaration) has its braced `if`-branch extracted as its own top-level span, leaving the braceless `else expr` continuation as dangling leading text at the very START of the NEXT real (braced) span rather than a genuine new statement -- `findParentIndent`'s backward scan, bounded by that next span's own `span.start`, defaults its `stmtStart`/anchor onto this dangling `else` when no other statement follows within the same span before the target brace. On round1 this `else` (with heavy inter-token padding aligning `else`/`throw`) was not the first token on its own physical line, so the existing "anchor must be first-token-on-its-line" check correctly returned `null`; on round2, a later phase's re-alignment happened to put that same `else` alone at the start of its own physical line, so the check instead returned that unrelated, EARLIER statement's own (wrong) indent. **Fix**: in `findParentIndent`'s anchor-selection step, when the anchor found is itself a dangling `else`/`catch`/`finally` keyword (Kotlin-gated, `lang.isKotlin`), skip forward past its one-line brace-less body to the next depth-0 NEWLINE and re-anchor on whatever real statement follows, rather than accepting the dangling keyword itself as an anchor. An earlier attempt instead skipped a `}` immediately followed by `else`/`catch`/`finally` during the BACKWARD scan itself was tried first and found ineffective -- that `}` sits outside the target span's own `span.start`..`openBraceIdx` window entirely (it belongs to the PREVIOUS span), so it was never visited by that scan; the forward anchor-side fix was required instead. A first attempt at a broader statement-boundary heuristic (recognizing any depth-0 NEWLINE followed by a declaration-starter keyword as a boundary) also fixed `BufferedChannel.kt` but regressed the pre-existing `real_code_regressions_33` fixture (a deliberately misindented nested `interface Builder` header whose closing brace intentionally aligns to its body's depth rather than its own shallower header column) -- reverted in favor of the narrower dangling-`else`/`catch`/`finally` fix, which does not touch that shape. | RDD_KEY_162 | Resolves RDD_KEY_161's last open question, `Kotlin/kotlinx.coroutines` `ChannelFlow.kt`'s declaration-alignment padding-width flap (the final file of the 10-file idempotency investigation): `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderAlignedGroup` padded `UndispatchedContextCollector`'s (typeless) `countOrElement` row out to match sibling `emitRef`'s `: suspend (T) -> Unit` type-column width, widening `emitRef`'s own line enough that a later pass wrapped its brace-bodied lambda initializer (`{ downstream.emit(it) }`); re-parsing that now-multi-line initializer on the next pass correctly (by existing design) bailed `emitRef` out of its alignment group via `spansMultipleLines`'s brace-depth check, collapsing `countOrElement`'s padding to a single space on round2 -- an inverted instance of the "later phase invalidates an earlier phase's physical-shape decision" bug family (RDD_KEY_136/152/158/159/160/161), here the *early* alignment phase destabilizing a *later* wrap phase instead of the reverse. **Fix**: plumbed indent-width/line-length-limit awareness into `renderAlignedGroup` -- new indent-width-aware `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule(Lang, int, int)` constructor (mirrors `KotlinSignatureRule`'s own shape), `DeclarationAlignmentRule.lineLengthLimit` raised private -> protected (RDD_KEY_139's loosen-then-extend precedent), `ScopePipeline` now stores its own `indentWidth` field and threads `depth` through `applyDeclarationsPass`/`applyKotlinDeclarationsPass` (same `depth * indentWidth` column estimate `GetterSetterRule`'s own width pre-check, RDD_KEY_139, already uses). `renderAlignedGroup(group, indent)` runs a fixed-point loop excluding any row from the shared column grid (rendering it solo instead) whenever its group-aligned width overflows `lineLengthLimit` AND its initializer is brace-bodied (new `hasBraceBodiedInit` -- a lambda/object/block, the only shape `spansMultipleLines`'s brace-depth check can ever bail on); a plain call/expression initializer (no brace) is never excluded regardless of length, since any future wrap of it lands strictly inside parens with no enclosing brace, which `spansMultipleLines`'s existing paren-depth carve-out (RDD_KEY_135) already keeps groupable/idempotent forever -- confirmed via the pre-existing `real_code_regressions_18` fixture (`display = if (...) full.substring(...) ...`), which an earlier attempt at this fix (excluding on group-aligned overflow alone, or on a "solo width already overflows" heuristic) wrongly regressed since it targeted the row causing the overflow rather than the row whose own future-wrap behavior actually risks a bail. `renderAlignedGroup(List<Row>)` (no indent arg) and the pre-indent-width constructors are kept as-is (`indentWidth <= 0` disables the check entirely), zero behavior change for any existing caller. New `test/real_code_regressions_45_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` (minimal `countOrElement`/`emitRef` repro). `make test`: 64/64 before, 65/65 after. | RDD_KEY_163 | `square/kotlinpoet` remaining Shape 1 idempotency-gap investigation (6 files: `CodeWriter.kt`/`LambdaTypeName.kt`/`MemberSpecHolder.kt`/`ParameterizedTypeName.kt`/`TypeVariableName.kt`/`WildcardTypeName.kt`): root-caused via debug prints in `ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass` (dumping `KotlinSignatureRule.renderWithTail`'s own decision) and `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` (dumping each candidate's name + rendered replacement), confirming two DISTINCT bugs in `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`, both stemming from its Kotlin function-signature candidates not being fully exempted the way a C/C++/Java forward declaration is. **Bug A** (`MemberSpecHolder.kt`'s `addProperties`/`addFunctions`, an expression-bodied `fun ...(...): T = apply { ... }`): `renderWithTail` correctly collapses the signature to one line each round (confirmed via debug print, byte-identical both rounds) since its own tier-1 fits-check deliberately excludes the untouched trailing `{ ... }` lambda body text; `enforceCallLineBreaking` then independently re-measures the WHOLE physical line (correctly, by design, to catch cases where that untouched trailing text pushes the total over the limit) via `lineEndIndex`, which simply stops at the first NEWLINE token -- but on a reformat (round2) an unrelated NESTED call inside that untouched trailing text (`apply { x.map(\n  y\n) }`, itself wrapped by this same pass on round1) already has an internal NEWLINE, so `lineEndIndex` stops there prematurely, undercounting the true rendered width and flipping the wrap-or-not verdict from round1's correct "too long, wrap" to round2's incorrect "looks like it fits". Confirmed empirically that RDD_KEY_160's `effectiveCallBaseIndent` fix does NOT touch this path (different method within `enforceCallLineBreaking`). **Fix**: new depth-aware `MiscRule.effectiveLineEndIndex` (tracks `(`/`[`/`{` nesting, only treats a NEWLINE as the true line end when depth is back to 0), used in place of `lineEndIndex` for this one fits-check; general (not Kotlin-gated) but confirmed inert for C/C++/Java via `make test` (65/65 before, unaffected). **Bug B** (the other 5 files, e.g. `LambdaTypeName.kt`'s `override fun copy(...): LambdaTypeName { ... }`, a genuine block-bodied signature with an explicit `: ReturnType` tail): `ScopePipeline`/`KotlinSignatureRule.render` already produces the fully correct column-padded, trailing-comma-preserved multi-line param list, but `enforceCallLineBreaking`'s own "is this actually a real signature, not a call" exemption (`afterClose is '{'` immediately after the closing `)`) only recognizes a signature with NO return type -- a `: ReturnType {` tail sits between `)` and `{`, so the exemption never fires, and this pass re-processes the already-rendered signature as an ordinary untyped call (per RDD_KEY_144's Kotlin `sigForRender = null` forcing), silently discarding the column padding and trailing comma. This is the exact mechanism RDD_KEY_149 (`square/okio`'s `RealBufferedSink.kt`/`FakeFileSystem.kt`) found and left deferred/unidentified -- now root-caused and resolved by the same fix. **Fix**: new `MiscRule.isKotlinReturnTypeThenBlockBody` lookahead (depth-aware scan from the top-level `:` after the candidate's `)`, returns true only if a top-level `{` is reached before any top-level `=`), extending the true-signature exemption to this shape; Kotlin-gated (`lang.isKotlin`), deliberately does NOT extend to a `: ReturnType = expr` tail (that shape still needs Bug A's fix, since it has genuine untouched trailing text `enforceCallLineBreaking` must still account for). New combined `test/real_code_regressions_46_inp.kt`/`_out.kt` covering both bugs. `make test`: 65/65 before fixture, 66/66 after. Re-verified all 6 Shape-1 files (`CodeWriter.kt`/`LambdaTypeName.kt`/`MemberSpecHolder.kt`/`ParameterizedTypeName.kt`/`TypeVariableName.kt`/`WildcardTypeName.kt`) against the real `square/kotlinpoet` tree (`indent-size=2`) now round1-vs-round2 clean; a fresh full-125-file round1/round2 re-run confirms zero new regressions, with exactly the 4 previously-tracked, unrelated Shape 2/3/4 files (`AbstractTypesTest.kt`, `ReflectiveClassInspector.kt`, `kmAnnotations.kt`, `KotlinPoetMetadataSpecsTest.kt`) remaining open. All debug instrumentation (env-gated prints in `ScopePipeline.applySignaturePass`/`Formatter.formatOne`/`MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`) removed before committing. |

| RDD_KEY_164 | `square/kotlinpoet` Shape 2 idempotency bug (`AbstractTypesTest.kt`'s `Parameterized<...> where ...` headerless generic class): `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceWhereClausePlacement` derived the wrapped `where` clause's base indent from `lineIndent(tokens, i)` -- the `where` token's own current physical line -- which on a reformat is already the previously-wrapped line, one level deeper than the true signature indent, compounding an extra indent level each round (non-idempotent). Root-caused via a minimal standalone repro (a nested `internal class Parameterized<...> where ...` inside an outer `class Container`) confirmed to reproduce round1-vs-round2, then bisected to `baseIndent`'s source. **Fix**: new `signatureLineIndent` helper mirroring `ScopePipeline.findParentIndent`'s "true statement start" posture -- scans backward from the `where` token tracking paren/bracket/`ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`/`ANGLE_BRACKET_CLOSE` depth, stopping at the nearest depth-0 `;`/`}`/`{`, rather than `lineStartIndex`'s "just back up one physical line" (which lands on a generic parameter list's own continuation line, not the class declaration's true first line, once the list is already broken across several lines). Confirmed fix via the real `AbstractTypesTest.kt` file (round1-vs-round2 now clean) plus the standalone repro. New `test/real_code_regressions_47_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 66/66 before, 67/67 after. | RDD_KEY_165 | `square/kotlinpoet` Shape 3 idempotency bug (2 files: `ReflectiveClassInspector.kt`'s `when(kotlin) { ... }` nested subject-form `when`, and `kmAnnotations.kt`'s `buildCodeBlock { ... }` plain trailing-lambda call, both used as a `when` branch's own multi-line body): `ScopePipeline.findMergingWhenBranchLineStart` (RDD_KEY_152) only detected a bare `when {` line as the shape `KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions`'s later arrow-merge pass would fold onto the preceding branch-label line -- a subject-form `when(subject) {` (extra `(...)` before the brace) and a plain call head (`identifier(...)? {`, no `when` keyword at all) both fell through unrecognized, so the nested scope's indent was computed against the pre-merge physical line on a fresh format but the post-merge line on a reformat, drifting the closing `}` by one indent level. **Fix**: generalized the lookahead -- accepts an optional balanced `(...)` span after `when` (subject form), and accepts a plain `IDENTIFIER` as the line's head token (not just the `when` keyword), while keeping the existing "immediately followed by openBrace" and "preceding line ends in a top-level `->`" guards unchanged. Confirmed via minimal standalone repros of both shapes plus the real files (`ReflectiveClassInspector.kt`/`kmAnnotations.kt`, both round1-vs-round2 clean after the fix) and a fresh full-125-file `square/kotlinpoet` re-run (only 1 file -- Shape 4, unrelated -- still diffs). New `test/real_code_regressions_48_inp.kt`/`_out.kt`. `make test`: 67/67 before, 68/68 after. Resolves `square/kotlinpoet`'s Shape 3; Shape 4 (`KotlinPoetMetadataSpecsTest.kt`) remains open. |

| RDD_KEY_166 | `square/kotlinpoet` Shape 4 idempotency bug (`KotlinPoetMetadataSpecsTest.kt`'s `val fooAliasData` declaration-alignment padding flap): root cause was in `TokenizerCore`'s named-construct-name tracking, not `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule` itself. The `class` KEYWORD-arms-`namedConstructKeywordSeen` branch fired unconditionally, including when `class` is the RHS of a Kotlin class-literal reflection expression (`Foo::class`), which is not an actual class declaration. Since a bare-expression statement using `::class` (e.g. `val typeSpec = TypeAliases::class.toTypeSpecWithTestHandler()`) has no following `;`/`}`/`{` to clear the flag before the statement ends, it stayed armed across the statement boundary and wrongly captured the *next* IDENTIFIER anywhere later in the file as `pendingNamedConstructName` -- here, a following `.first { ... }` lambda's enclosing function, which then wrongly gained named-construct treatment (blank line after `{`, closing name comment) it should never have received, in turn perturbing `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s padding-exclusion fixed-point logic for the next `val` group on a reformat. Root-caused via ~10 rounds of minimal-repro bisection (narrowed from the full real file down to two `val` declarations plus one preceding sibling `val ... = X::class...` line) before finding the exact tokenizer branch via source inspection. **Fix**: `TokenizerCore`'s `class`-keyword-arms branch now checks a new `isPrecededByDoubleColon()` helper (true iff the most recent significant token tracked so far is the `::` operator) and skips arming in that case, for Kotlin only. While root-causing/verifying this fix two more bugs surfaced and were fixed in the same pass, both in `KotlinSpecificRule.signatureLineIndent` (the RDD_KEY_164 helper), found because a from-clean rebuild (`rm -rf target/test-out && make test`) exposed a stale-output false-PASS that had been masking them: (1) a genuine `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: -1` crash when a `where`-clause-bearing statement sits at the very top of the file with no preceding `;`/`}`/`{` boundary at all (`stmtStart` stays `0`, and the old sentinel `stmtStart == 0 ? -1 : stmtStart - 1` fed a literal `-1` into `nextSignificantIndex`'s `for (int i = from; ...)` loop, which does not special-case negative `from`); (2) confirmed via a fixed version that merely avoided the crash (scanning forward from `stmtStart` itself) that this broke `real_code_regressions_47`'s Shape 2 fixture -- the helper's original, correct semantics anchor on the *boundary token's own physical line* (the enclosing scope's indent, since `where`'s wrap indent is computed as `baseIndent + indentUnit`, i.e. one level under the *enclosing* scope, matching the signature's own level, not one level under the signature's own already-one-level-deeper line) rather than skipping past the boundary to the new statement's own first token. Final fix keeps the original `stmtStart - 1` boundary-token anchor when a boundary was found, only falling back to scanning from index `0` (never `-1`) when none was (`stmtStart == 0`). Confirmed all three fixes via the real `KotlinPoetMetadataSpecsTest.kt` file (round1-vs-round2 now clean) and a fresh full-125-file `square/kotlinpoet` re-run: `diff -rq round1 round2` now empty across the entire tree -- `square/kotlinpoet` is now fully round1-vs-round2 idempotent. | RDD_KEY_167 | `JXM_CFMT_CFG` top-of-file placement semantics, resolved with user before implementation began (per STATE_COMMON.md's design note flagging this as needing confirmation): the directive is detected via a raw-text scan for its own literal `//%`/`/*%` opening marker and must be its OWN separate comment -- it is never recognized if pasted inside the interior of another comment (e.g. merged into the existing copyright-header block comment's prose), since the scanner only matches the marker at a comment's own opening delimiter. Placement rule: "before the first non-comment/non-blank token" (not literal line 1) -- a leading copyright header (or any other leading comment/blank line) above the directive does not count as a violation, as long as the `JXM_CFMT_CFG` comment is itself a distinct comment token appearing anywhere in that leading preamble, before the first real code token. Matches how other header-comment-aware passes in this formatter already work. To be documented in `README.md` alongside the rest of the `JXM_CFMT_CFG` feature once implemented. |

| RDD_KEY_168 | `in_file_config_*.hpp` fixture design, resolved with user before building fixtures: `header-guard-rename=on` is untestable via this harness's `_inp`/`_out` diff convention -- `deriveGuardName` derives the guard name from the literal invocation path, so the forward pass (`test/in_file_config_inp.hpp`) and the idempotency pass (`test/in_file_config_out.hpp`, a genuinely different filename) always derive two different guard names, meaning no single committed `_out.hpp` text can satisfy both diffs simultaneously -- a real, previously-undiscovered interaction between `header-guard-rename` and the fixture-naming convention itself, unrelated to the CFG-directive feature. **Decision: drop `header-guard-rename` from the fixture's key list; use `format-macros=off` as the C/C++-specific distinguishing key instead** (also rejected `line-endings=crlf` for this slot -- this repo's `core.autocrlf=input` would silently normalize a committed CRLF fixture back to LF without an added `.gitattributes` override, judged not worth the extra machinery for one test). `format-macros=off` has a second benefit beyond avoiding the guard-rename conflict: the `test` Makefile target unconditionally exports `JXMAKE_CODE_FORMATTER_FORMAT_MACROS=on` for every test run, so a fixture directive setting `format-macros=off` also proves the in-file layer's documented highest-priority override of env vars specifically (if the override wiring were broken and the env var leaked through, the macro-value columns would wrongly align, a visible, easy-to-diff signal). |

| RDD_KEY_169 | `ericniebler/range-v3` item 20 bug (a) RESOLVED, the idempotency divergence left open in `utility/any.hpp`/`iterator/common_iterator.hpp`/`meta.hpp` (RDD_KEY-adjacent Open Questions entry, no prior key -- two earlier LOW-effort sessions tried and reverted `isGenericSafeToken` OP-whitelist tweaks without success). Root-caused via temporary instrumentation (env-gated debug prints in `BlockStructureRule.addClosingComments`'s push/pop loop and a full token-stream dump, both removed before finishing): `enforceKAndRBraceStyle`'s `qualifiesForKAndR` glues a named construct's (`class`/`struct`/`enum`/`namespace`) body `{` onto the end of the immediately preceding physical line unconditionally once it qualifies for K&R placement, with no check on what that preceding line actually is. When the construct's base-clause/underlying-type sits behind a preprocessor conditional (`struct any\n  #if X\n  : base\n  #endif\n{`), the brace legitimately follows a bare `#endif` line, and the glue collapses the intervening newline into a space, producing `#endif {` on one physical line -- which is never valid C/C++ layout on its own but was silently produced by this rewrite. The very next retokenization (`addClosingComments`'s own `tokenizer.apply(text)` call, both within round1 itself and again on round2) hits `TokenizerCore.emitPreprocessor`, which correctly (per its own opaque-to-end-of-line contract) swallows the `{` into the `#endif` PREPROCESSOR token's text, permanently dropping that `{` as a distinct token. This desyncs `addClosingComments`'s brace/frame stack for the remainder of the scan: the construct's real closing `}` instead pops the next frame up (typically the enclosing `namespace`), producing symptom (1)'s misindented/mislabeled `}; // namespace X` closing-comment lines. Symptom (2) (`meta::if_c<std::is_reference<T>() || copyable<T>, T>`'s inner angle brackets getting wrongly spaced on round2) turned out to share this exact root cause rather than being separate -- the stray unclosed frame shifts what every subsequent brace-depth-derived heuristic downstream (including `TokenizerCore.reclassifyAngleBrackets`'s notion of "top-level") sees for the rest of the scan; the original `isGenericSafeToken`/`invalidateAll(openStack)` lead from the two prior sessions was a red herring -- that code was never buggy, it was just fed an already brace-desynced token stream. **Fix**: `enforceKAndRBraceStyle` now checks the nearest real preceding token via the existing `prevSignificantIndex` helper and skips the K&R glue (leaves the `{` on its own line) whenever that token is a `TokenType.PREPROCESSOR` directive, regardless of what kind of construct the brace belongs to -- gluing real code onto a preprocessor-directive line is never a safe rewrite, since a later retokenization pass has no way to distinguish an accidental glue artifact from an intentional one-line macro body. `qualifiesForKAndR`'s own construct-classification logic is untouched. Verified: minimal repros (`struct`/`enum class` with an `#if`/`#endif`-guarded base-clause before `{`) now leave the brace on its own line and are round1-vs-round2 clean; the 3 originally-flagged real files now produce byte-identical round1/round2 output (`diff` empty on all three, both symptoms gone); `make test` 75/75 forward + 75/75 idempotency before and after, zero regressions; a fresh full round1/round2 pass over the entire 311-file `include/range/v3/` tree is completely `diff -rq`-empty (whole tree idempotent, not just the 3 files); a `g++ -std=c++20 -fsyntax-only` syntax-check pass over all 311 files shows the identical single pre-existing failure (`detail/epilogue.hpp`, `#error`s when compiled standalone without its paired `prologue.hpp`, a header-pairing artifact unrelated to formatting) with the same 2-error output in both the unformatted baseline and the formatted tree; every other file compiles clean in both. Fixed in `BlockStructureRule.enforceKAndRBraceStyle`. Closes item 20 bug (a) entirely -- no files remain open in the `ericniebler/range-v3` idempotency investigation. |

| RDD_KEY_170 | microsoft/proxy dogfood (item 22 of the C/C++ real-code-testing queue): found and fixed 3 bugs in `CppSpecificRule.enforceRequiresClausePlacement` (the pass placing a trailing C++20 `requires` clause after a function/template signature's closing `)`, either inline or wrapped to its own line one level under the declaration's indent). Root cause (a)/(b), an idempotency bug: the pass computed both its wrap-vs-inline fit check and its own-line indent (`baseIndent`) from the physical line the `requires`-preceding `)` currently sits on. When the parameter list itself is multi-line, that `)` can land on very different lines across passes -- on a fresh, unformatted source it may sit at the end of a deeply source-aligned continuation line (giving a bogus, deeply-indented `requires` line matching that column, e.g. 38 spaces, seen in `allocate_proxy`'s `std::initializer_list<U>` overload in `include/proxy/v4/proxy.h`); on a reformat of already-formatted output the parameter-wrapping pass (`MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`, which runs AFTER this pass in `Formatter`'s Phase 1/2 ordering) has already left `)` alone on a short, dedented line, so the fit check now reports "fits inline" and glues `requires` back onto `)`'s own line -- a stable round1-vs-round2 divergence, not a one-off glitch. A second wrinkle (b) compounded this: a chained trailing specifier between the parameter list and `requires` (most commonly `noexcept(...)`, seen in the `proxy(std::in_place_type_t<P>, std::initializer_list<U>, Args&&...)` constructor overload) meant the paren directly preceding `requires` wasn't even the parameter list's own close paren, but the specifier's -- so a naive "one level of `matchParenBackward`" fix still measured off the wrong (and itself sometimes awkwardly pre-wrapped by an unrelated bug) line. Fix: derive both `baseIndent` and the fit-check text from the parameter list's own OPENING paren's line instead of the closing paren's -- found by unwinding backward through any chain of specifier close-parens (a `)` immediately preceding an opening paren, or a `noexcept` keyword immediately preceding one, each themselves preceded by another `)`) until reaching a paren whose predecessor is the actual function name, not another specifier. This measurement only depends on token content collapsed to one line, never on incidental wrapping already present in the input, so it's stable across arbitrarily many format passes. Root cause (c), a compile-breaking bug (not idempotency): `format_impl`-style methods in `include/proxy/v4/proxy_fmt.h`/`proxy.h` write their `requires(...)` constraint expression's body behind a preprocessor conditional (`requires(\n#if __cpp_lib_format_ranges >= 202207L\n std::formattable<T, CharT>\n#else\n std::is_default_constructible_v<std::formatter<T, CharT>>\n#endif\n)`) -- `enforceRequiresClausePlacement`'s `collapseToOneLine` helper (used for both the inline-fit render and the own-line render's clause text) unconditionally space-joins every token in the clause onto one physical line, which is illegal for `#if`/`#else`/`#endif` (a preprocessor directive is only ever recognized at the very start of a physical line) -- the collapsed output left `#if`/`#else`/`#endif` and the surrounding expression fragments all on one line, which `clang++ -std=c++23 -fsyntax-only` then rejected with a cascade of "expected expression"/"no member named"/"expected '}'" errors stemming from the malformed `requires` clause corrupting everything downstream in the same translation unit. Fixed with the same "leave completely untouched" posture already used for a comment or frozen span inside the clause's replaced range: a new `hasPreprocessorBetween` helper scans for any `TokenType.PREPROCESSOR` token in `(closeParenIdx, clauseEndIdx)` and, if found, skips the rewrite for that `requires` occurrence entirely (verbatim source form preserved, matching the existing comment/frozen-span bail-outs' contract). Verified: (a)/(b) — minimal repros of both the initializer_list-overload and the `noexcept`-chained-constructor shapes are now round1-vs-round2 byte-identical; a full round1/round2 pass over the whole `microsoft/proxy` tree (28 `.h`/`.cpp` files under `include/`, `tests/`, `benchmarks/`, `tools/`, formatted in place with `--lang cpp` preserving original filenames so `#include "proxy_macros.h"`-style relative includes still resolve for the compile check) is completely `diff -rq`-empty. (c) — `clang++ -std=c++23 -stdlib=libc++ -fsyntax-only` (`~/xsdk/clang22/LLVM-22.1.8-Linux-X64/bin/clang++`, stderr piped through `grep -v 'no version information available'` to filter the harmless libstdc++ symbol-versioning warning) on `include/proxy/proxy.h` and `include/proxy/v4/proxy.h` shows 0 errors both on the unformatted baseline (`/tmp/proxy`) and on the round1-formatted tree, matching exactly (the pre-fix round1 tree had introduced 13 new formatter-caused errors on each of those two files, now gone); `proxy_macros.h`/`v4/proxy_macros.h` also 0 errors both before/after (they don't use `requires`). `proxy_fmt.h` itself can't be syntax-checked standalone in either baseline or round1 (it `#error`s without an external `{fmt}` library dependency not available on this machine -- out of scope per STATE_COMMON.md's "no full linking/external deps" posture -- but its `requires(...)`-with-`#if` shape is exercised directly by the new fixture instead). `make test`: 77/77 forward + 77/77 idempotency (up from 76/76 pre-fixture, zero regressions). Fixture: `test/real_code_regressions_53_{inp,out}.cpp`, combining all 3 bug shapes in one file (the `allocate_proxy` initializer_list overload for (a), the `noexcept`-chained constructor for (b), and a `format_impl` example with an `#if`/`#else`/`#endif`-guarded `requires` body for (c), adapted/simplified from the real `microsoft/proxy` source under the standard fixture copyright header). All 3 fixes are localized entirely to `CppSpecificRule.enforceRequiresClausePlacement` and its two new/modified private helpers (`hasPreprocessorBetween` new; the paren-unwinding loop and `declLineStartIdx`/`baseIndent` derivation replacing the old single `lineIndent(tokens, closeParenIdx)` call) -- no other rule class touched, no Java/Kotlin-shared code touched. |

| RDD_KEY_176 | `arrow-kt/arrow` dogfood, bug 4 (the `Either.kt` `zipOrAccumulate` bug deferred at the end of the prior session, tracked in STATE_KOTLIN.md): the Kotlin compile-error was mistakenly attributed to `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` by the deferring note's own guess, but debug-print tracing across `Formatter.java`'s pipeline showed the fused-without-`;`-separator text was already present *before* `enforceCallLineBreaking` ever ran, and the fused text was independently confirmed invalid Kotlin via `kotlin_sc` even in isolation. Fixed by reusing the existing `containsMultilineNestedBrace` helper as a bail-out guard in `collapseBracelessBody`, mirroring the analogous protection already present in the braced-body path; this is inherently Kotlin-only (both call sites of `collapseBracelessBody` are already Kotlin-gated). | RDD_KEY_177 | `arrow-kt/arrow` dogfood, `Comparison.kt`'s `sort2` idempotency flap -- the last open item, closes the entire arrow-kt/arrow investigation. Root cause confirmed as diagnosed in the prior session's deferral note: `BlockStructureRule.collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s `isKotlinExpressionIf` exemption only guarded a *parenthesized* expression-position `if` (`kotlinParenDepth > 0`); an unparenthesized depth-0 if-expression used as an entire expression-bodied function's whole body (`private inline fun <A> sort2(a: A, b: A, leq: (A, A) -> Boolean) = if (leq(a, b)) Pair(a, b) else Pair(b, a)`) was still treated as an ordinary collapsible control-flow statement and unconditionally re-flattened on every pass -- fine on a fresh format (the whole signature+body is still one long physical line at that point, so the too-long line correctly drives `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` to wrap everything consistently), but on a reformat of already-wrapped output, this pass re-collapsed just the body in isolation onto its own now-short line, which then measured as fitting and stayed collapsed -- two different stable states depending on pass ordering, not a fixed point. Fixed in two parts, both additive/Kotlin-gated, no C/C++/Java behavior change: (1) extended the exemption to a depth-0 `if` directly preceded (skipping non-significant tokens) by a bare `=` that is itself a *function's* expression-body tail, not a `val`/`var` declaration's initializer -- new `isFunctionExprBodyEquals(tokens, eqIdx)` walks backward from the `=` tracking bracket depth to the enclosing statement's own leading keyword, returning true only for a depth-0 `fun` (stopping at a depth-0 `val`/`var`/`;`/NEWLINE otherwise); this correctly leaves `val display = if (...) ... else ...` (`real_code_regressions_18`) still going through the existing collapse-then-rewrap path unaffected, since that shape's leading keyword is `val`, not `fun`. (2) The exempted if's paired bare `else` arm is a structurally separate branch of the same dispatch loop (fires on the `else` keyword token, not the `if` token, so `isKotlinExpressionIf`'s per-token computation never applies to it) and has no condition of its own to run the same check against -- without a fix here it still mis-collapsed the exempted if's `else` body back onto one line on a reformat (found while verifying part (1) alone was insufficient: `sort2.kt` round1 wrapped `else Pair(b, a)` onto 3 lines via `enforceCallLineBreaking`, but round2 collapsed it straight back via this unguarded `else` branch). Fixed by a `pendingKotlinExprBodyElse` boolean, set when the main loop passes an `if` token found exempt via case (1) and consumed (cleared) by that same if's paired bare `else`, which now skips the collapse and falls through to the default single-token append when the flag is set. Verified: minimal repro `sort2.kt` round1/round2 byte-identical; a fresh full-scope 63-file `arrow-core`+`arrow-optics` `commonMain` reformat (config `indent-size=2`) is round1/round2 byte-identical across the *whole* scope (`diff -rq` empty except for the injected `.jxmake-code-formatter` config file itself, not part of either output tree); `kotlin_sc` reports 0 syntax errors across all 63 files (same as before this fix -- this was always a pure idempotency flap, never a compile error); `make test` 88/88 clean (87/87 before the new fixture), zero regressions. Fixture: `test/real_code_regressions_64_{inp,out}.kt`. Closes the arrow-kt/arrow dogfood investigation entirely. |
| RDD_KEY_171 | Local `src/jxm` dogfood (candidate 23): idempotency bug in `TokenizerCore.reclassifyAngleBrackets`. The method already handled `<` (single open), `>` (single close), and `>>` (splitting a literal 2-char right-shift-lexed token across 2 valid opens, or 1 valid open + 1 leftover literal `>`), but had no case at all for a literal `>>>` token -- needed whenever a real generic type nests three levels deep with no intervening space at the close, e.g. `HashMap<String, HashMap<String, ArrayList<String>>>>` (found in `MapList.java`/`StackList.java`/`xb/XCom.java`). Round1 formats an unformatted/differently-spaced source correctly (each `<`/`>` is lexed as a separate single-char OP token by the raw tokenizer, so the existing single-`<`/single-`>` cases in the loop handle each one individually, converting all three opens/closes to `ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`/`ANGLE_BRACKET_CLOSE` and rendering them tight with no inserted spaces). But re-tokenizing round1's own tight output on round2, the raw lexer's greedy multi-char-operator matching (`>>>` is in the fixed operator-token list, matched before falling back to single-char `>`) now lexes the same three adjacent `>` characters as one `>>>` OP token instead of three separate `>` tokens. That token fell through every explicit case in `reclassifyAngleBrackets` to the final `isGenericSafeToken` fallback, which does not recognize `>>>` as generic-safe, so it called `invalidateAll(openStack)` -- every `<` on that open-stack (all three) was left as an ordinary OP token instead of being retyped to `ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`, and downstream rendering then treated them as ordinary relational-operator `<`s, padding spaces around them (`HashMap < String, HashMap < String, ArrayList < String >>>`). Fixed by adding an explicit `>>>` case, generalizing the existing `>>` case's split logic one level further: if 3+ opens are on the stack, pop and validate all 3, retype all 3 opens plus split the `>>>` token into 3 consecutive `ANGLE_BRACKET_CLOSE` tokens (distributing the 3-char text as 1 char on the first, empty string on the other 2, mirroring the existing `>>`-with-2-opens case's empty-string-on-second-token precedent so total rendered text length is preserved); if exactly 2 opens are on the stack, pop and validate both, retype both opens, and split the `>>>` text as 2 `ANGLE_BRACKET_CLOSE` tokens (1 char each) plus 1 leftover literal `>` OP token (1 char) for the untracked outer bracket, total 3 chars preserved; if exactly 1 open is on the stack, pop and validate it, retype it, and split as 1 `ANGLE_BRACKET_CLOSE` token (1 char) plus 1 leftover literal `>>` OP token (2 chars), total 3 chars preserved. Each branch requires careful `shiftSigAfter` bookkeeping when inserting 2 new tokens into the underlying token list in one pass: the second `shiftSigAfter` call must use the sig-array position of the just-inserted first new token (`s + 1`) as its `afterPos`, not the original triggering token's position (`s`) again, or the sig array's index bookkeeping double-shifts/misplaces the second insertion (verified by manual trace of the `tokens.add`/`sig` index arithmetic, not just by output diffing, since a sig-array bug of this kind can silently produce plausible-looking-but-wrong output on inputs with fewer than 2 consecutive uses of the newly-added branch). Verified: targeted round1/round2 on `MapList.java`, `StackList.java`, `xb/XCom.java`, `tool/JSONEncoderLite.java` now byte-identical for the previously-diverging lines; `make test` 89/89 forward + 89/89 idempotency (up from 88/88, the new fixture below). `TokenizerCore.java` is listed in `STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md`'s Project Layout as a shared (not `rules/`-package) class also used by the Kotlin job, so `make test`'s full run (which also exercises every Kotlin fixture) was used as the shared-class regression check per STATE_COMMON.md/this candidate's task instructions, rather than a narrower Java-only check -- no Kotlin fixture regressed. Fixture: `test/real_code_regressions_65_{inp,out}.java` (combined with RDD_KEY_172 below in the same file). |
| RDD_KEY_172 | Local `src/jxm` dogfood (candidate 23): idempotency bug in `JavaSpecificRule.isSingleLineBody`, the fits-under-`lineLengthLimit` prediction used by `enforceMethodDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle` to decide whether a method-definition one-liner (`Type name(params) { ...single physical line... }`) should stay K&R even though `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` (a separate, later pass) may go on to break a call inside its body across multiple lines anyway once the whole line is measured against the limit -- this predict-before-committing design is documented inline as existing specifically to avoid a K&R/Allman flip-flop (round1 keeps `{` K&R with a broken-up body underneath; round2 sees the now-genuinely-multi-line body and would otherwise flip to Allman). The prediction's own width measurement, however, only summed the token span from the method's first significant token (`lineStart`, deliberately excluding the line's own leading indentation) through the closing `}` -- it neither added the physical line's leading indentation width nor accounted for any trailing same-line `//` comment following the `}` (a common shape here: hand-aligned §14 getter/setter-style comment columns, e.g. `private static _RWxMem _reg(final _RWx reg) { return new _RWxMem(0b000, reg, Reg.NA, 0); } //    Wx`). `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s own fit-check, by contrast, measures `baseIndent + collapseToOneLine(...)` over the *entire* physical line including any trailing comment. Whenever the omitted indent-plus-comment width alone was enough to push an otherwise-under-limit line over `lineLengthLimit` (as in the `_reg` example: without indent+comment the span measures 90 chars, comfortably under the 100-char default limit, but the real physical line including 8-space indent and the trailing `//    Wx` comment is 107 chars), the two passes disagreed: `isSingleLineBody` predicted "fits" and kept `{` K&R, `enforceCallLineBreaking` correctly saw the real line was too long and broke the call body across multiple lines anyway, producing a K&R signature with a broken-up multi-line body underneath on round1; reformatting that already-broken round1 output on round2, `isSingleLineBody`'s very first check (an unconditional scan for any `NEWLINE` token inside the brace span) immediately returned false since the body was now genuinely multi-line, so it correctly went Allman on round2 -- a K&R-vs-Allman idempotency divergence, found in `ugc/PIC16BitEPC.java`'s `_reg`/`ind`/`ind_mm`/`ind_pp`, `ugc/ProgBootSerial.java`'s three `_setSerialRxTimeout_MS_*` one-liners, and `ugc/ProgSWD.java`'s `xviGet`. Fixed in two parts: (1) added the line's own leading indentation width (via the existing `lineIndent` helper, already used elsewhere in this class for Allman-`{` re-indentation) to the running `width` total; (2) extended the scanned span past `closeBraceIdx` to include any trailing same-line comment (scanning forward token-by-token until a `NEWLINE` or end-of-list, matching `effectiveLineEndIndex`'s own style elsewhere in `MiscRule`), and switched the width computation from summing each token's raw `text.length()` (which, run early in the pipeline before general whitespace normalization, could still include un-collapsed multi-space padding literally present in the *original* source between tokens -- e.g. hand-authored column-alignment spacing before a comment -- and so could just as easily *overstate* the width for a line that, once actually rendered/collapsed, fits fine) to a whitespace-run-collapsing scheme that treats any consecutive run of `WHITESPACE`/`NEWLINE` tokens as exactly one space, mirroring `MiscRule.collapseToOneLine`'s own collapsing behavior exactly so the two passes' fit-checks agree not just directionally but numerically. (Caught during fix verification: a first attempt that added the trailing-comment span's raw character lengths without this collapsing regressed a previously-non-diverging sibling one-liner, `Expr.lit(final int value)`, whose original source had heavy hand-alignment padding before its own trailing comment that inflated the naive raw-length sum well past the limit even though the line, once collapsed/rendered, comfortably fits -- resolved by the collapsing fix, verified via a temporary `JXM_DEBUG_ISB` env-var-gated stderr print of the computed width/limit/text for every `isSingleLineBody` invocation against the real file, confirming an exact match to `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s own textual fit measurement before the debug code was removed.) Verified: targeted round1/round2 on `ugc/PIC16BitEPC.java`, `ugc/ProgBootSerial.java`, `ugc/ProgPIC_SIX_REGOUT.java` (already passing before this fix, confirmed still passing after), `ugc/ProgSWD.java` all byte-identical round1 vs round2; `make test` 89/89 forward + 89/89 idempotency (shared fixture with RDD_KEY_171, see below), zero regressions including all pre-existing Java one-liner/§14-group fixtures. `JavaSpecificRule.java` is Java-only (not a class shared with the Kotlin job -- `KotlinSpecificRule.java` carries its own independent, separately-maintained copy of `isSingleLineBody`, not touched by this fix), but `make test` was still run as the full regression check per this candidate's task instructions. Fixture: `test/real_code_regressions_65_{inp,out}.java` (combined with RDD_KEY_171 above in the same file, per STATE_COMMON.md's "combine multiple bugs in the same text fixture if possible" guidance). |
| RDD_KEY_178 | Local `src/jxm` dogfood (candidate 23): two related bugs in `MiscRule`'s STYLE.md §8 multi-line parameter-list renderer -- `render()`'s inline signature branch and its near-identical multi-line-declaration counterpart, both around `p.leadingComment` (a standalone comment preceding a parameter, distinct from `p.comment`, a same-line trailing comment) rendered via a shared `leadPrefix`/`typeCell` pattern. Found in `ugc/fl/SWDFlashLoader.java`'s `Specifier` constructor (a `////...////` banner comment used as a section divider between logically-grouped parameters) and `ugc/stm32xf/STM32QSPI.java`'s `newQSPICmd` (a `// Instruction` label comment before its first parameter). Bug (1): the pre-fix code always inlined `p.leadingComment.text` as a text prefix on the same physical output line as the following parameter's type+name (`lines.add(paramIndent + leadPrefix + typeCell + nameText)` with `leadPrefix = p.leadingComment.text + " "`), with no check on the comment's token type. A `//` line comment, unlike a self-terminating `/* ... */` block comment, extends to end-of-physical-line -- so this silently swallowed the parameter's own declaration into the comment text, and, once that corrupted output was re-tokenized on a later pass, cascaded into swallowing whatever parameter followed too (compile-breaking, not just a missed reformat). Fixed by checking `p.leadingComment.type == TokenType.COMMENT_LINE` and, when true, emitting the leading comment on its own separate preceding output line (`lines.add(paramIndent + p.leadingComment.text)`) instead of inlining it; block comments still inline exactly as before via the same `leadPrefix`/`typeCell` path when `leadingIsLineComment` is false. Bug (2), found only after fixing (1) and re-running the full targeted verification against both real files: `STM32QSPI.newQSPICmd`'s `final InstMode instMode,` (immediately following the reattached `// Instruction` leading comment) rendered as `final         InstModeinstMode,` on round2 -- type and name merged with zero space. Root cause: the shared column width used to align every parameter's name after its type (`typeColWidth`, derived from `maxTypeLen`, the longest `typeText` among params) is computed by a loop that explicitly `continue`s past (excludes) any parameter with a non-null `p.leadingComment` of *either* comment type, on the reasoning that such a parameter's own type+name isn't part of the aligned column in the original per-param-group layout -- but bug (1)'s fix still renders that same excluded parameter's type+name through the ordinary `padRight(typeText, typeColWidth)` path once its leading comment is no longer inlined, and `padRight` is a documented no-op once the string already reaches or exceeds the target width. `InstMode` alone is short, but combined with its `final ` prefix and the fact that the *other* params in that signature (`long`, `boolean`) are shorter still, `typeColWidth` (13/14, from the longest non-excluded type) ended up smaller than `"final InstMode"`'s own 15-char length, so `padRight` added zero trailing spaces, merging type and name into one identifier-looking token on the next tokenize pass. Fixed by changing both occurrences' `padRight(typeText, typeColWidth)` call to `padRight(typeText, Math.max(typeColWidth, typeText.length() + 1))`, guaranteeing at least one space regardless of how the shared column width compares to this specific (uncounted-toward-that-width) parameter's own type length; ordinary non-excluded params are unaffected since `typeColWidth` is already `maxTypeLen + 1`, which by construction already exceeds every *included* param's own type length by at least 1. `MiscRule.java` is a shared class (also instantiated by the Kotlin job via `new MiscRule(lang, ...)` in both `Formatter.java` and `ScopePipeline.java`, per `STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md`'s Project Layout), so `make test`'s full suite (covering every registered Kotlin fixture too) was used as the shared-class regression check; zero Kotlin fixtures regressed. Verified: targeted round1/round2 on real copies of `ugc/fl/SWDFlashLoader.java` and `ugc/stm32xf/STM32QSPI.java`, both byte-identical round1 vs round2 after both fixes (an intermediate check after fixing only bug (1) still showed the STM32QSPI divergence, which is what surfaced bug (2)); `make test` 90/90 forward + 90/90 idempotency (up from 89/89, the new fixture below), zero regressions. While constructing a minimal fixture for this pair of bugs, a third, narrower, pre-existing and NOT-fixed ambiguity was found and deliberately routed around rather than chased: `parseSignature`'s post-comma-split reattach loop (an already-existing, untouched-by-this-fix heuristic that reattaches a comment token immediately following a comma back onto the *previous* parameter as its trailing comment, to correctly handle `int b,   // second` shapes) cannot distinguish that same-line-trailing-comment shape from a *standalone* comment on its own source line intended to lead the *next* parameter, when the preceding parameter has no trailing comment of its own already -- both shapes look identical in the token stream once `significantWithComments` strips `WHITESPACE`/`NEWLINE` tokens before this reattach logic runs, i.e. the ambiguity is a genuine loss of line-boundary information earlier in the pipeline, not something fixable at this call site. This does not corrupt output (the misattributed comment still renders safely as an ordinary trailing comment on the previous parameter, just not on the parameter the original author probably intended it to visually introduce) and never manifested in the actual `src/jxm` tree, since both real occurrences happen to have their own preceding same-line trailing comment already (masking the ambiguity); the fixture below was deliberately shaped to match the real files' exact pattern (each preceding param carrying its own trailing comment) to avoid exercising this separate, out-of-scope, non-corrupting ambiguity. Fixture: `test/real_code_regressions_66_{inp,out}.java`. |
| RDD_KEY_179 | Scaffold kickoff (C++26/data-formats/JS-TS/Python3), C++26 language-selection mechanism: `.cpp`/`.hpp` extensions are shared with the existing, already-implemented C++20/17 baseline (`STYLE_CPP20.md`), so `Lang.infer` cannot auto-detect C++26 from extension alone -- the style doc (`STYLE_CPP26.md`) itself specifies no selection mechanism. Resolved by reusing the existing `--lang`/server `lang=` explicit-override escape valve (already precedented for other extension ambiguities, e.g. `.h` defaulting to C but overridable to C++) rather than inventing a new flag shape: `--lang cpp26` / `lang=cpp26` selects C++26 mode explicitly; omitting it (or any other `.cpp`/`.hpp` file with no `--lang` override) keeps resolving to `"cpp"` (C++20/17 baseline), byte-for-byte unchanged. `Lang.isScaffoldOnly("cpp26")` is true, so both CLI and server currently route it straight to `UnsupportedLanguageException` (scaffold-only this session, no real C++26 formatting logic yet) -- see `STATE_CPP26.md`. |
| RDD_KEY_180 | **REVERSES RDD_KEY_179** -- C++26 is not a separate selectable language after all. Per user review: this project's own precedent (`STYLE_CPP20.md` extending `STYLE_C_CPP.md`) never introduced a separate `isCpp20`/`--lang cpp20` selector -- C++20 support was folded additively into the existing, single `"cpp"` pipeline as more rule coverage on the same shared extension. `cpp26` broke that pattern by inventing a distinct language identity (`Lang.isCpp26`, `SCAFFOLD_ONLY_LANGUAGES` entry, explicit-only `--lang cpp26`/`lang=cpp26` selection) for something that is architecturally the same situation as C++20 was: more C++ grammar coverage on the same `.cpp`/`.hpp` files, not a distinct dialect requiring caller opt-in. Reverted: `Lang.isCpp26` field, `cpp26` from `SCAFFOLD_ONLY_LANGUAGES`/`isScaffoldOnly`, and the `--lang cpp26` explicit-selection story removed entirely from `Lang.java` (`Main.java`/`ServerMode.java` needed no changes -- they consume `Lang.isScaffoldOnly`/`SCAFFOLD_ONLY_LANGUAGES` generically, not by name). C++26 now has no scaffold entry at all; when its real rule coverage is implemented, it lands directly in the existing `"cpp"`-gated rule classes (`CppSpecificRule.java` etc.), same as every other C++ dialect extension before it. `STYLE_CPP26.md`/`STATE_CPP26.md` content updated to match (no `--lang cpp26` references). |

| RDD_KEY_190 | `FormatterCore.forLanguage` dispatch open question raised while implementing real JSON/JSON5 logic (STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md checklist's first item): `Lang.isCurly`/`isIndentBased`/`isTagBased` cover every existing `Formatter*` sibling, but JSON/JSON5 (and CSS) are none of the three, and RDD_KEY_189 already ruled out a `TokenizerFlat` sibling for the tokenizer side specifically to avoid a family with too few members.
